# Scooby Project Source Pack: Index and Research Map

**Snapshot:** 13 November 2026. **Purpose:** one navigable Project source for the full archive, with three transcript volumes and one combined results/attachments volume. The branch archive remains the permanent record; this pack is a searchable convenience copy.

## Use this pack

1. Search this index for a topic, chat title, source-result filename, or response-card title.
2. Open the linked Project conversation or exact message. The adjacent GitHub link points to the preserved archive record.
3. Use the transcript volumes for full message text and the results volume for the 36 uploaded Project result files plus six chat input attachments.
4. Preserve the distinction between a record, a research finding, an inference, and a hypothesis. A transcript or saved response is not independent verification.

Historical messages and prompts are preserved as records, not current instructions or authorization. This is an inventory and retrieval aid; it does not certify that every planned Deep Research task completed or reconcile claims with the wider repository.

## Inventory

- 117 Project chats, all reader pages complete (`hasMore: false`).
- 137 Project Sources entries: 36 downloadable result files and 101 saved original-response cards.
- 6 files attached as chat inputs (four text/Markdown files and two PDFs).
- 10 rendered message captures: six full renderings supplement reader-truncated messages; four preserve saved responses omitted from the reader turn payload.
- Archived history pagination covered 1,052 entries and found no Scooby-assigned archived chats.

## Transcript volume map

| Volume | Chats | First / last Project history order |
|---|---:|---|
| `01_Scooby_Chat_Transcripts_Part_1.md` | 47 | 1 / 47 |
| `02_Scooby_Chat_Transcripts_Part_2.md` | 46 | 48 / 93 |
| `03_Scooby_Chat_Transcripts_Part_3.md` | 24 | 94 / 117 |

Each transcript volume preserves every user and assistant message body with turn timestamps and message IDs. The original JSON reader records remain available by GitHub link. Full rendered text for truncated/omitted messages is attached to the matching chat section.

## Known scope limits

- The four regex-debugging conversations for R17–R20 document prompt-extraction failures and debugging. This archive does not establish whether those tasks later received completed Deep Research runs. Separate result files exist; prompt-to-result completion has not been audited.
- The 36 result files are preserved verbatim but have not been substantively reconciled against existing repository records here. Filename similarity is not proof of a match.
- The chat history is a 13 November snapshot; later Project changes are not included until the pack is regenerated.

**Permanent archive:** <https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/tree/scooby-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/research/scooby-chatgpt-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12>

Each item retains its original title, source type, conversation association where applicable, original SHA-256, and GitHub record link. Text payload bodies are verbatim inside fenced blocks. PDFs are text-extracted page by page with Poppler `pdftotext -layout`; the original PDF bytes remain available from GitHub.

````
Source: Deep Research chat “Unchanged Gate Report,” matched to R48 by its prompt (WTC 7 motion and sound), Nov. 12, 2026.

Gate status: No qualifying new input verified. No newly available native recording or authenticated calibration record sufficient to advance the motion/acoustic analysis was identified.

The source check found that the 656 transition fits (Sept. 20, 2026) are arithmetic on published 2023 measurements, not new original-frame measurements; the multipoint motion review (Sept. 19) still lacks authenticated original cadence, camera-to-frame mapping, and calibration uncertainty; the acoustic audit consists of compilation-level digital checks while original soundstreams and recorder-specific detectability remain unresolved; and the Nov. 11 structural audit §4 added no new audiovisual measurement. The Nov. 6 structural review identifies the same source-to-camera, pixel-measurement, and synchronization gaps.

A qualifying addition would be an identified original continuous recording with verifiable acquisition timing and source/copy lineage, or authenticated original-linked frame coordinates, camera calibration, or recorder response data. Acoustic comparison additionally requires recording conditions, receiver characteristics, and a defensible event-specific detectability threshold.

Existing motion and sound findings remain unchanged; neither the NIST fire-progression explanation nor an impulsive alternative gains support from this source check. No new metrology, synchronized event reconstruction, structural simulation, or causal inference was performed. No requests, contacts, fees, or repository changes were made.
````

````
Source: Deep Research chat “Gated Evidence Review” (prompt verified as R46, Mineta/Cheney), Nov. 12, 2026.

Status: GATED — required new evidence was not identified. The November 11 brief retained N12 as unresolved; no completed N12 analysis or newly available contemporaneous record independently established the disputed exchange’s time, aircraft, instruction, or recipient.

The May 23, 2003 Mineta testimony establishes his recollection of the aide–Cheney exchange and his interpretation that the instruction concerned shootdown. It does not independently establish the instruction’s wording, original authorization, or exact event time. The July 22, 2004 Commission report places Cheney in the shelter conference room shortly before 10:00 while acknowledging conflicting evidence. Commission chapter 1, note 216, cites leads including White House switchboard logs, contemporaneous notes, an air-threat conference recording, Secret Service logs, and four White House Military Office logs; these are references, not newly retrieved originals. NARA’s Sept. 11, 2026 notice of declassified Commission interviews identifies retrospective interviews, not newly authenticated operational logs.

Both pre-Pentagon-impact and post-impact reconstructions remain unresolved at the disputed-exchange level. Existing evidence does not establish a stand-down, a particular shootdown authorization, or deception. The needed discriminator is an authenticated, independently timed Sept. 11 operational record identifying the aircraft, aide’s question, actual order wording, authorized recipient, and event time. No fresh chronology reconstruction or dependent order analysis was performed. No requests, contacts, fees, or pending matters were changed.
````

````
Gate result: Limited new primary evidence is available. No completed Able Danger follow-up report was found. The newly accessible input was a public transcript reproduction of the actual Feb. 15, 2006 House Armed Services hearing; it was not verified against the full 165-page GPO edition.

The testimony sharpens handling accounts: J.D. Smith said he retained a defective print of the alleged Atta chart until its destruction during a 2004 office move and described acquiring photographs through outside sources, without identifying an authenticated photograph, dated source record, or surviving original chart. Erik Kleinsmith distinguished deletion of LIWA’s primary and secondary datasets from data transferred elsewhere and denied participating in destruction at the Garland facility. These are witness accounts, not independent authentication of the chart’s alleged contents.

The DoD Inspector General’s Sept. 18, 2006 report identifies a signed Aug. 30, 2000 Phillpott memorandum and a surviving comparison chart. Phillpott later rejected his belief that Atta appeared on that chart. The original attachments and distribution records remain unavailable. The IG documents a disputed photograph supplier chain; the original image remains unavailable.

Crane’s four allegations remain unadjudicated in the accessible record. No verified executed affidavit, ICIG intake acknowledgment, or identifiable merits disposition was found. A Sept. 2026 book listing an appendix titled “John R Crane Affidavit” was not inspected and does not authenticate a signature or establish a misconduct finding.

The attached report contains an artifact/version graph, witness-source dependence table, allegation-by-allegation tests, and separate custody/destruction histories. The hearing testimony permits a limited update; central original-record and Crane-disposition gaps remain open. No contacts, requests, fees, or pending matters were changed.
````

````
tsapps.nist.gov

nvlpubs.nist.gov

NIST associates the stairwell disturbance with the 10:28 a.m. North Tower collapse, after the 9:59 a.m. South Tower collapse. Whether an earlier, separate disturbance occurred remains unverified.

2. Michael Hess — WTC 7

Source and date

	

Event information

	

Timing independence

Reported UPN broadcast and later rescue-time critique

3. William Rodriguez — North Tower

Source and date

	

Event information

	

Timing independence




CNN, September 11, 2001, 1:30 p.m. ET, original published segment transcript

	

Describes two successive rumbles, a severely burned man and subsequent rescue efforts

	

Contemporary account, but no independently timed disturbance




Commission interview, reportedly June 8, 2004

	

A public reference identifies an interview conducted by George Delgrosso and James Miller

	

The complete underlying record could not be authenticated and inspected here

CNN's complete published transcript confirms Rodriguez's reported sequence but does not explicitly establish that either rumble preceded the 8:46:40 a.m. aircraft impact. His estimated 15–20-minute gap concerns later activity, not a measured interval between the initial noises. 
Transcripts
Lead Stories

Bounded conclusion — unchanged

The decisive uncertainties remain:

Jennings/Hess: Whether the stairwell disturbance occurred before 9:59 a.m., between 9:59 and 10:28 a.m., or at approximately 10:28 a.m.; and when the two men were actually rescued.

Rodriguez: Whether the first reported basement rumble preceded, accompanied or followed the 8:46:40 a.m. North Tower impact.

The original continuous broadcasts, independently timed rescue/dispatch records, and authenticated complete Commission interview are still insufficiently available for the requested comparison.

Fuel/fire effects, falling debris, structural movement and an unidentified disturbance therefore remain possible explanations at different points in these event chains. None can be identified solely from the word explosion.

No new reliable timing anchor changed the existing assessment. The evidence gate remains closed, so the dependent chronology and mechanism analysis stops here. No contacts, requests, fees or pending matters were changed.
````

### 21. R28-—-Iraq-Policy-Source-Audit-—-Research-Result-(Nov-12,-2026).txt

- Type: Project uploaded result file

- Original payload: <https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/scooby-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/research/scooby-chatgpt-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/project-source-files/R28-%E2%80%94-Iraq-Policy-Source-Audit-%E2%80%94-Research-Result-%28Nov-12%2C-2026%29.txt>

````
Scooby R28 — Iraq policy source audit

The key distinction is between documented early interest in military action against Iraq and an authenticated presidential decision to invade.

The November 5 Scooby review already established that Iraq regime change was under consideration before September 11, and that military options were discussed immediately afterward. The November 11 follow-up retained two missing primary records rather than treating the first-week chronology as settled. 
iraq_policy_evidence_review_2026-11-05.pdf
 
Scooby_Deep_Research_Followups_2026-11-11.txt

An important source distinction is now confirmed: the 9/11 Commission cites its September 17 NSC conclusions memorandum in chapter 10, note 71, but attributes the specific oil-field planning detail to Condoleezza Rice's April 8, 2004 testimony and Bob Woodward's Plan of Attack, p. 22, in note 72. The oil-field assertion therefore cannot be described as a verified quotation from the September 17 memorandum. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

Document and version ledger

Record

	

Original, version and distribution status

	

Evidentiary significance




July 27, 2001 — Rumsfeld to Rice

	

Four-page working paper, 4:47 p.m.; addressed to Rice, copied to Cheney and Powell. Original, pp. 1–4.

	

Advocates renewed Iraq policy deliberations, including regime change, military options and possible dialogue. Not an invasion decision.




September 11 — Cambone notes

	

Contemporaneous handwritten notes, marked 2:40 p.m. Internal record; no demonstrated presidential approval or distribution. Original, p. 1.

	

Records Rumsfeld considering action involving Saddam Hussein alongside bin Laden. An option raised after the attacks began.




September 16 — Rice NSC memorandum

	

Cited in Commission chapter 10, note 51, with named senior recipients. Full original and attachments not acquired.

	

A distinct document. Its recipient list cannot be assigned to September 17.




September 17 — NSC conclusions

	

Identified in Commission notes 49–50, 52 and 71. Original, attachments, version history and distribution not recovered.

	

The Commission reports contingent Iraq planning. Exact instructions and their recipients remain unverified at original-document level.




September 18 — Kurtz to Rice survey

	

Titled Survey of Intelligence Information on Any Iraq Involvement in the September 11 Attacks; Commission note 62. Original and alleged successive drafts not recovered.

	

The Commission reports no compelling Iraq–9/11 case. Cannot independently assess exact wording, revisions or why a draft was returned.




November 27 — Rumsfeld–Franks notes

	

Three-page original planning document. Original, pp. 1–3.

	

Examines triggers, military targets, oil fields and postwar administration. Page 3 distinguishes the rough concept from complete execution planning.




August–September 2002 — Policy-office presentations

	

DOD IG reconstructs draft presentations on August 8, August 15 and September 16. An undated original slide extract is available; complete authenticated versions of all three decks were not obtained.

	

Establishes an alternative intelligence argument and documented differences in presentation. Does not establish each author's intent or every recipient's knowledge.

Sources: original Defense Department records and National Security Archive reproductions; Commission chapter 10 and notes; DOD IG Report 07-INTEL-04, especially pp. 9–19. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

Findings that change or qualify the assessment

1. The September 17 oil-field detail remains less securely sourced than the broader planning account.

Rice's April 8, 2004 public testimony supports the proposition that Bush requested contingency planning should Iraq act against American interests or evidence emerge connecting it to the attacks.

However, her published testimony does not mention oil fields. Although Commission note 72 cites both Rice and Woodward, the specific oil-field detail cannot be verified from Rice's testimony. Woodward's cited page and the original September 17 conclusions were not independently compared here. 
9-11commission.gov

The November 27 original separately establishes later consideration of oil fields. It cannot retrospectively establish the contents of the September 17 instruction.

2. The first-week record contains genuine management disagreement.

The Commission describes Wolfowitz advocating Iraq, Powell opposing the diversion, and Bush prioritizing Afghanistan. Its account combines contemporaneous documents with later interviews.

Rumsfeld's September 11 notes are direct evidence of his consideration of Iraq, but they do not independently establish agreement by Bush or other principals.

Likewise, the Commission's account of the September 18 survey and its disputed revision history shares a documentary source and conflicting retrospective witnesses. Repetition in subsequent histories is not independent corroboration. 
911commission.gov
+1

3. The 2002 briefing dispute supports a narrower conclusion than deliberate fabrication.

The DOD IG identified variations in briefings presented to different audiences and criticized the failure to communicate important differences from intelligence-community assessments.

Its chronology also documents earlier intelligence objections, including challenges to numerous briefing claims. This strengthens the finding that senior policy recipients were presented with a contested analysis.

But the IG did not determine that every alternative judgment was knowingly false. Management defended the briefings as authorized criticism of prevailing assessments. An undated slide and the IG's reconstructed presentation history cannot, by themselves, establish an author's deliberate deception on a particular date. 
nsarchive2.gwu.edu
+1

Revised assessment

Strengthened: A pre-existing Iraq objective, immediate consideration of expanded military action after September 11, increasingly concrete planning by November, and substantial subsequent disputes over intelligence presentation.

Qualified: The extent of presidential commitment during September 12–18, the wording and recipients of the September 17 instruction, the survey's alleged revision history, and the provenance of the oil-field detail.

Not established: An unconditional September 17 invasion order, deliberate falsification by a particular 2002 briefing author, or advance participation in arranging September 11.

The original September 17 conclusions and September 18 survey are the decisive unresolved inputs. Dependent wording, distribution, knowledge and revision tests stop there.

I found no later completed Scooby policy audit resolving those two originals. This was a direct public-source continuation; a separately billed Deep Research run was not initiated under the no-fee restriction. No contacts, requests, legal filings, payments or project changes were made.
````

### 22. R27-—-N19-P23-Alleged-Payment-Audit-—-Research-Result-(Nov-12,-2026).txt

- Type: Project uploaded result file
- Bytes: 9536
- SHA-256: `3fa5bd7ebaf67056844dbbeed4d00e84220928a6097041bc27941a8e5c9f490c`
- Original payload: <https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/scooby-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/research/scooby-chatgpt-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/project-source-files/R27-%E2%80%94-N19-P23-Alleged-Payment-Audit-%E2%80%94-Research-Result-%28Nov-12%2C-2026%29.txt>

````
Scooby N19/P23 — Alleged ISI-directed $100,000 payment

Preliminary finding: The original allegation is documented, but the alleged transaction and instruction remain unverified.

The November 11 financial review explicitly excluded this issue. The Scooby source audit identifies N19 as a separate exhibit investigation, and I have not identified a completed report resolving it in the accessible project material.

1. Origin and source dependency

The earliest specific allegation identified in the prior review is Manoj Joshi's October 9, 2001 Times of India article, India helped FBI trace ISI-terrorist links.

It asserts that Ahmad Umar Sheikh transferred $100,000 to Mohammed Atta at the direction of ISI director-general Mahmud Ahmad. It further attributes the alleged discovery to Indian intelligence assistance and an FBI investigation.

The article cites unnamed Indian government sources. It provides neither a bank record nor an identified FBI investigative finding. Its description of American confirmation is itself part of the allegation, not an independently obtained American confirmation. 
India News - Times of India

The distinction is important: evidence that a transfer occurred would not automatically establish the claimed sender, the ISI director's involvement, or advance institutional knowledge of the attacks.

Earlier reporting establishes a more complicated chronology

The October 9 article was not the first public mention of the money trail.

October 1, 2001: CNN reported approximately $100,000 reaching Atta through Florida banks, purportedly via Pakistan, according to investigative sources. The account described transfers over time, not an authenticated single $100,000 transaction. 
Transcripts
+1

October 7, 2001: CNN identified Ahmad Omar Saeed Sheikh as the believed sender, citing an unnamed senior law-enforcement source. 
Transcripts

October 9, 2001: The Times of India added the specific assertion that Mahmud Ahmad had instructed Sheikh. 
India News - Times of India

These are at least two distinct reporting channels, but their underlying investigative information may overlap. The unnamed sources cannot presently be counted as independent corroboration.

2. Authenticated financing exhibits

The federal court's original Moussaoui prosecution exhibit index identifies relevant bank and money-transfer records. The original images establish the following distinct transactions:

Record

	

Amount and date

	

Documentary finding




BR00028A

	

$9,985; report run July 19, 2000

	

Originator text: Isam Mansur. Beneficiary: Atta/Shehhi account. UAE exchange route. Bates M-MMB-00002187




BR00028B

	

$9,485; report run August 7, 2000

	

Originator text: Isam Mansour. Shehhi beneficiary. Bates M-MMB-00002185




FO11001

	

$5,000; June 2000

	

Western Union record identifying Isam Mansar and Marwan al-Shehhi




FO11006

	

$5,000; September 8, 2001

	

Mohamed Atta sends money outward to Mustafa Ahmad in the UAE

The first two dates are report-processing dates, not independently verified initiation dates. The last transaction's direction is especially important: it cannot be recast as a $100,000 inbound payment.

None of these four exhibits independently establishes the alleged ISI-directed transfer. They also do not establish that no additional transfer occurred.

3. A consequential FBI clarification

The most important additional source is the 9/11 Commission staff's 2004 Terrorist Financing Monograph, Appendix A, printed page 144, footnote 163.

It explicitly addresses John Pistole's July 31, 2003 testimony about tracing funds to Pakistani accounts. According to the Commission staff, the FBI subsequently clarified that Pistole meant funds traced to Khalid Sheikh Mohammed in Pakistan, and that no actual Pakistani bank accounts had been identified. 
Terrorist Financing

This materially limits the use of Pistole's testimony as corroboration of the alleged $100,000 wire. It does not exclude an unrecorded cash transfer, an intermediary transaction, or a separate account that investigators failed to identify.

The same monograph, printed pages 133–135, reconstructs five documented transfers totaling $114,500 to Atta and Shehhi during 2000, attributed to Ali Abdul Aziz Ali. Four were bank-to-bank transfers; one used Western Union. The investigators relied on UAE transaction paperwork, identification information, interviews and Ali's statements. These transactions supply a concrete alternative explanation for early reports of approximately $100,000 in incoming funding, although they do not establish how those early reports arose. 
Terrorist Financing
+1

The monograph's conclusion that it found no evidence of foreign-government funding is relevant contrary evidence. Its staff reviewed FBI financial analyses and underlying bank records, but the public report does not show a separate, transaction-specific investigation of Mahmud Ahmad's alleged instruction.

4. FBI records resolve the two earlier bank exhibits

A more detailed original is the FBI's Summary of PENTTBOM Investigation, dated February 29, 2004, printed pages 16–17, Bates 000003860–3861. It separates initiation from posting and accounts for the $15 difference in the two examined bank exhibits:

Transfer initiated

	

Gross sent

Isam Mansour

$9,500

	

$9,485 on August 7

	

Isam Mansur




August 29, 2000

	

$20,000

	

$19,985 on August 30

	

Mr. Ali




September 17, 2000

	

$70,000

	

$69,985 on September 18

	

Hani at Fawaz Trading

All four went through the UAE Exchange Centre to Shehhi's Florida SunTrust account, jointly held with Atta. The FBI attributed the sender aliases to Ali Abdul Aziz Ali. Four gross transfers total $109,500; net credits total $109,440. 
vault.fbi.gov

This is independently checkable against the earlier FBI Director's September 26, 2002 congressional statement, which identifies the same four-transfer total and sender aliases. That earlier statement derives from the FBI investigation; it should not be counted as a second independent account audit. 
irp.fas.org

There is also a particularly relevant contemporary warning against conflating identities. CNN's October 4, 2001 investigative broadcast explicitly questioned whether Ahmad Umar Saeed Sheikh and the individual using the name Mustafa Muhammad Ahmad were the same person. Its reporter said investigators were uncertain. Subsequent evidence identifies Ali Abdul Aziz Ali and Mustafa al-Hawsawi as distinct participants in the known financing network; it does not retrospectively prove which individual each early unnamed source meant. 
Transcripts

5. Claim-to-transaction matrix

The original public source establishes that an accusation was made. It does not establish any of these six underlying propositions.

6. Source-dependency map and stopping rule

Allegation chain

October 2001 CNN reports of approximately $100,000

Uncertain identification of Sheikh / Mustafa Ahmad

October 9 Times of India allegation of Mahmud Ahmad's instruction

Underlying transfer and tasking records not recovered

Documented financing chain

UAE bank and remittance records from 2000 → FBI investigative summaries → Moussaoui exhibits and testimony → Commission staff analysis.

Repeated descriptions of these records are not independent confirmation of the separate ISI allegation.

One additional documentary discrepancy remains relevant: the May 16, 2002 White House transcript records a reporter's question about money wired to Pakistan, whereas the earlier allegation concerned money sent from Pakistan. The question also supplies a September 10 date, but no transaction record. Its wording cannot settle the transfer's existence or direction. 
georgewbush-whitehouse.archives.gov

Final assessment: The documented 2000 financing provides a plausible source for confusion in early reports, but the actual journalistic source chain has not been reconciled. Neither that alternative nor the ISI-directed explanation is established for the alleged separate transaction.

The first decisive requirement is an authenticated transfer or originating investigative referral. Until then, attribution to Mahmud Ahmad must stop.

Download the complete N19/P23 evidence audit — claim matrix, exhibit inventory, source map and original links

This is a bounded public-source audit, not a certified search of all agency holdings or a newly initiated paid Deep Research run. No contacts, filings, requests, fees or pending matters were changed.
````

````
Scooby — United Defense / Carlyle accounting investigation

Research update • November 12, 2026 • R26 / FIN-P05

1. Original credit agreement recovered

The most important new finding is that the August 13, 2001 credit agreement is publicly available, contrary to the acquisition gap recorded in Scooby's November 11 financial review.

United Defense — Credit Agreement, Exhibit 4.4

Original SEC filing • October 22, 2001 S-1 • Accession 0000928385-01-502154

The agreement identifies Iron Horse Investors, United Defense Industries, lending institutions and the administrative and syndication agents. It is dated August 13, 2001, and establishes an $800 million credit facility. A July 2, 2002 amended-and-restated version is also publicly available. 
dex44.txt
+1

The August 13 versus August 31 discrepancy is not yet resolved. The original agreement uses August 13, as does the December 2001 prospectus's refinancing description. UDI's 2004 Form 10-K instead dates the refinancing August 31. The original transaction may have involved distinct agreement, funding or accounting dates, but closing and bank-disbursement records are needed to establish that explanation. 
ds1a.txt
+2

The agreement's availability is a material improvement in document provenance. It does not, by itself, identify the eventual recipients of the dividend, prove that every advance occurred on the agreement date, or establish any relationship to advance knowledge of September 11.

2. Reconstructed transactions

The review establishes the following flows. Amounts attributed to TCG Holdings are calculated from SEC beneficial-ownership tables; they are not verified distributions to Carlyle's individual investors.

The 1997 acquisition figure comes from UDI's 2001 S-1/A, Note 3. The offering figures follow from the original share counts and net offering prices. 
ds1a.txt
+3

The original filings also reveal a separate transaction: UDI purchased United States Marine Repair from Carlyle-related owners on July 2, 2002. UDI reported consideration of $305.4 million, including approximately $105 million of debt repayment; its later filing reports $306.9 million. The entire acquisition price therefore cannot be attributed to Carlyle shareholders. 
sec.gov
+1

The strongest verified share reconciliation is:

11,222,672
−

=
4,436,051.
11,222,672−6,786,621=4,436,051.

These are TCG's reported shares before February 2004, shares sold in February, and shares remaining for the April exit. 
sec.gov
+1

Fee accounting also matters: UDI reported $2 million annually paid for 1999–2001, another $3.5 million in August 2001 recapitalization fees, and a final $3 million payment for January 2002–March 2004 services. The intervening accrued fees must not be counted again as separate cash payments. 
sec.gov
+1

Two further findings constrain the analysis:

The intervening sales are substantial. Carlyle and affiliated holders reported selling 11,805,336 shares in August 2003 and 1,491,346 in November. Their reporting scope differs from the TCG-only figures; the proceeds cannot be assigned to one fund without the individual selling schedules. 
sec.gov

The business did not enjoy uniform protection. Crusader was cancelled in 2002, approximately 180 employees were affected, and termination-cost settlement remained incomplete in June 2004. This provides specific adverse evidence against a guaranteed-profit interpretation. 
sec.gov

The reported bin Laden family investment remains a separate question. Contemporary reporting describes a roughly $2 million fund interest and an October 2001 exit, but I found no authenticated subscription, capital-account or redemption record identifying the legal recipient. Neither UDI ownership tables nor repeated newspaper accounts establish that an individual received later offering proceeds. 
chron.com
+1
````

- Original payload: <https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/scooby-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/research/scooby-chatgpt-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/project-source-files/R25-%E2%80%94-Contractor-Procurement-Audit-%E2%80%94-Research-Result-%28Nov-12%2C-2026%29.txt>

````
Scooby — Contractor procurement audit
1. Established findings

The November 11 financial review identified substantial post-attack contracting advantages for KBR, alongside documented procurement-control failures. The original GAO findings establish two separate issues:

November 2002 LOGCAP planning order: GAO determined that the Iraq oil-contingency planning assignment exceeded the existing contract's scope. DOD disputed that interpretation and cited a contemporaneous legal opinion.

March 2003 Restore Iraqi Oil (RIO I) award: GAO concluded that the subsequent sole-source award generally complied with applicable competition requirements. That determination does not establish that the selection process was optimal, free of influence, or adequately supervised.

These conclusions are compatible. A legally defensible later award does not retroactively settle the earlier scope dispute. 
gao.gov
+1

GAO's July 31, 2007 audit independently establishes serious weaknesses in cost negotiation and award-fee administration. Its findings must be evaluated separately from claims about political influence or advance knowledge of September 11. 
U.S. GAO
+1

2. Questioned costs and fees

Amounts in USD millions; original: GAO-07-839, July 31, 2007, Tables 1–3, pp. 12–13, 21, 24.

Accounting category

	

Amount

	

Evidentiary meaning




DCAA questioned costs

	

$221.418m

	

Costs challenged by auditors, not established theft




DCAA sustained costs

	

Approximately $26m

	

Negotiated cost reductions attributable to audit findings




Reduction to fee-calculation base

	

$112.163m

	

Excluded from fee calculations; not a cash recovery




Calculated fee reduction

	

$5.826m

	

Lower fees resulting from revised bases




Maximum possible award fee

	

$108.631m

	

Conditional performance incentive




Award fee reported paid

	

$56.515m

	

Government-reported fee payment, distinct from the 2% fixed fee

gao.gov
+3

A consequential distinction is that DOD accepted payment of most questioned costs while reducing the base on which fees were calculated. Neither the $112.163 million adjustment nor the $5.826 million fee effect represents recovery of the entire questioned amount.

GAO also documented that all ten task orders were definitized more than 180 days after work began. The first formal award-fee board convened in July 2004, after nearly all fieldwork was finished; contractor notification followed in January 2005. The available documentation did not permit GAO to reconstruct every criterion-specific fee-board score. 
Department of Defense
+1

3. Additional findings

Three documentary details sharpen the existing assessment.

The February 4, 2003 LOGCAP contingency plan references the November 8, 2002 planning statement of work and its December 20 extension. This strengthens the dated chain connecting the planning assignment to KBR's subsequent readiness advantage. 
esd.whs.mil

A September 18, 2012 DoD Inspector General report, drawing on an earlier investigation, identifies an April 30, 2003 modification expanding RIO task order 3 beyond emergency repairs into broader oil-facility restoration. This introduces a separate postaward scope issue; it does not establish that the original award was unlawful. 
2012 Update

GAO's $26.158 million sustained-cost figure includes roughly $17 million removed from earlier proposals and approximately $9 million comprising disallowed and reallocated costs. It cannot be described as $26.158 million recovered from KBR. 
gao.gov

4. Research packet and remaining limitation

Download the contractor procurement audit and authorization-to-payment ledger

The report contains the dated contract chronology, source-dependency analysis, ten-task-order award-fee ledger, accounting reconciliation, competing explanations, and missing-originals register.

The requested complete packet index remains unfinished. The Judicial Watch PDF is publicly identified as 117 pages, but I could not independently retrieve and hash its bytes against the recorded SHA-256. Pages 36–117 therefore remain unverified, and I cannot determine whether they change the earlier conclusions. 
judicialwatch.org

No contractor closeout margin was calculated without original cost and payment records. No fee-bearing Deep Research run, external contact, records request, or amendment to a pending matter was undertaken.

The supported conclusion remains documented postattack commercial advantage with significant procurement-control weaknesses. Evidence establishing preattack participation is still absent from the reviewed records; that is an evidentiary limit, not a finding that every possible connection has been excluded.
````

````
app.midpage.ai
+1

The Port Authority's 2007 audited Note K reports approximately $4.57 billion recovered and approximately $2.3 billion already applied to specified obligations and expenses. This is a cumulative use-of-funds disclosure, not an additional payment or an itemized cash-flow statement. 
panynj.gov

2. Payments, assignments and ownership

The clearest insurer-level reconciliation comes from Zurich's June 13, 2008 prospectus, p. 108. It reports $76 million previously paid and a final April 27, 2007 wire of $19.34 million, comprising $15.34 million in coverage and $4 million in interest. These are components of the insurer's cumulative recovery, not additional payments to add to the main-site total. The actual bank remittance remains unavailable. 
dl.bourse.lu

The December 23, 2003 restructuring also requires distinguishing entities. A federal court describes the Port Authority purchasing Westfield's retail-lessee membership for $140 million. That transaction does not establish payment to Silverstein personally. The separately reported $229 million cash arrangement, including a $98.5 million equity return, remains unverified against executed closing statements and partner capital accounts. 
HallApproved.com
+1

Two further accounting qualifications remain:

The 2012 and 2013 judicial accounts differ by $6 in the WTCP allocation. The workbook preserves both values.

The approximate $4.55 billion announcement and $4.57 billion Port Authority disclosure differ from the precise 2013 payment figure by approximately $31.79 million and $11.79 million, respectively. Missing payment schedules prevent assigning those differences to specific components.

3. Separate recoveries and legal qualifications

WTC 7's approximately $819 million settlement and subsequent $11.94 million receipt belong to a separate insurance program. The approximately $95 million aviation settlement in 2017 is a separate tort recovery. Neither belongs in the main-site property-insurance total.

The Second Circuit's September 17, 2015 partial vacatur also prevents treating the 2013 valuation and interest judgment as a final determination of full economic compensation. 
app.midpage.ai
+1

Deliverables

Download the cash-flow and ownership workbook

Five sheets: reconciliation formulas, dated flows, ownership and return-calculation gate, missing originals, and source register.

Read the detailed source audit

Source dates and page locators, payment distinctions, competing interpretations, and outstanding dependencies.

Final determination: The available evidence supports substantial insurance receipts, documented debt and rebuilding obligations, and a plausible but unverified investor-capital return. It supports neither a calculated personal windfall nor a conclusion of zero personal benefit.

A complete return calculation remains blocked by missing Defense Exhibit B4, joint stipulation, remittances, 2003 closing records, partnership distributions, liabilities and terminal valuations.

No new paid Deep Research run was launched. This was a public-source documentary audit. No contacts, requests, fees or pending-matter changes were made.
````

````
Scooby brief 19 — WTC insurance underwriting audit

The recovered evidence establishes substantive pre-September 11 insurance negotiations, but does not establish that the policy changes were designed to exploit advance knowledge of the attacks. Nor does it establish that every change was commercially routine.

The strongest documented explanation for the central form switch is that Travelers required its own insurance form to participate in the primary coverage layer. Willis accommodated that requirement, but other underwriters did not uniformly accept the replacement.

The September 26, 2003 and October 18, 2006 Second Circuit decisions provide important contemporaneous-document quotations and judicial findings. They do not substitute for the underlying email attachments, signed binders or complete underwriting files. 
law.justia.com
+1

Prior-work and source status

The November 11 brief 19 correctly separated underwriting purpose from subsequent owner benefit. The earlier insurance Deep Research entry available in Scooby confirms initiation, but does not contain a completed report for this narrower underwriting audit.

I also verified a potentially consequential retrieval limitation: the NYC Tax Appeals Tribunal identifies Taxpayer Exhibits 1–4 as the July 16, 2001 leases and Exhibits 5–8 as amendments effective July 24. The published October 12, 2011 decision quotes selected clauses, but is not the executed exhibit package. The 2012 Donovan v. Hauben proceeding further documents that public access to those tax-trial exhibits was denied under tax-confidentiality rules. 
nyc.gov
+1

+2

The verdicts were genuinely mixed. In Phase I, the jury found WilProp governed 20 Lloyd's syndicates and nine of twelve participating insurers. In Phase II, the remaining nine insurers' contracts were found to contemplate two occurrences. That is incompatible with either a universal two-occurrence arrangement or uniform acceptance of WilProp. 
OpenJurist

A consequential additional lead

A February 2002 New York Observer investigation reported examining a July 12, 2001 broker memorandum indicating lenders initially sought $5 billion of coverage, while the buyer resisted the cost and ultimately obtained approximately $3.5 billion.

This is evidence worth testing against the original memo and lender records. It is not a verified $5 billion contractual covenant, but it challenges an interpretation that the insured amount was deliberately maximized without ordinary financing pressure. 
Observer

Unresolved evidence and conclusion

The decisive originals remain unrecovered: Exhibit A1 with both attachments; the executed July 16 leases and July 24 amendments; GMAC's signed covenants and valuation work; complete insurer slips and endorsements; and suitable contemporaneous comparison placements.

Those gaps prevent a valid conclusion that the transaction was exceptionally structured for an anticipated attack. They equally prevent certifying that every clause, limit and negotiation was commercially ordinary.

The complete audit includes the dated proposer–recipient–assent ledger, clause comparisons, source links, dependency analysis and exact unresolved-document list.
````

````
# Scooby — Pentagon Evidence Custody, N15 / November 11 brief 30

**Research date:** November 12, 2026  
**Scope:** Public and already-held sources; receipt-to-release and original-media provenance, not a general camera survey.  
**Prior-work index:** *Scooby_Deep_Research_Followups_2026-11-11.txt*, brief 30, N15.  
**Baseline:** *pentagon_video_evidence_research.pdf*, November 7, 2026, all 8 pages reviewed.

## Executive finding

The strongest new source recovery is Jacqueline Maguire's original signed September 7, 2005 declaration. It substantiates the court's account of the 85-record search and says the released impact CD-ROM contained **copies of two time-lapse recordings** from Pentagon parking-lot cameras. The public trial exhibit contains two descriptions with inconsistent clip numbering, but the numbers do **not** demonstrate a missing item. The September 12, 2001 FD-597 lists 16 surveillance tapes, a CCTVware recorder/hard-drive unit (model 951-0008), and a key received from Greg Goff. No public item-level crosswalk recovered here proves those particular tapes or that unit generated the P200022 CD-ROM or joins each to its current disposition. Acquisition/capture-rate and clock-correction findings remain blocked by missing native records.

A search of the accessible completed research index and linked available results found no completed N15 follow-up beyond the November 7 baseline. This is an original-source desk continuation, **not a claim that a separate billed Deep Research service ran**.

## Original documents and versions verified

1. **FBI FD-597, September 12, 2001, file 265D-NY-280350, NCT0073581–82.** The cropped FBI-form original reproduced on **November 7 report p. 5**, visually checked, has the 'Received From' box selected; names **Greg Goff**; lists '16 video surveillance tapes from 9-11-01,' '1 CCTVware digital recording unit model no. 951-0008 (hard drive),' and '1 small gold key to hard drive.' The original two-number NCT document package is indexed in the 9/11 Commission's Department of Defense index. **Restriction:** The full underlying page/package was not reacquired in this pass; the index is metadata, not a second independent inventory. Original scan locator: https://archive.org/download/51087055MstrB153RDOD04019658FdrFBIPropertySheet16911PentagonSurveillanceTapes021/51087055-Mstr-B153-RDOD04019658-Fdr-FBI-Property-Sheet-16-9-11-Pentagon-Surveillance-Tapes-021.pdf .
2. **Jacqueline Maguire declaration**, executed **September 7, 2005**, filed **September 9**, in *Bingham v. DOJ*, D.D.C. Civil No. 05-0475, Document 16, 6 substantive pages. Actual scan: https://www.judicialwatch.org/archive/2006/doubletreeaffadavit.pdf . Source filename is misleading: the declaration covers the 85-record search. **Paragraph 4** says the FBI located a CD-ROM containing copies of **two time-lapse recordings** made by Pentagon parking-lot security cameras. **Paragraphs 11–14** describe the pool, exclusions and personally viewed records. **Paragraph 15** expressly includes the Citgo recording in the 85; **paragraph 16** identifies separate Doubletree/Sheraton search results. The scanned pages were read visually.
3. ***Bingham v. DOJ*, memorandum opinion, December 29, 2006**, 8-page court PDF; pertinent PDF **pp. 2–3, 6–8**. https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/USCOURTS-dcd-1_05-cv-00475/pdf/USCOURTS-dcd-1_05-cv-00475-0.pdf . This is a legal account and decision relying on Maguire's declaration, not independent confirmation of 85 contents.
4. **Moussaoui prosecution exhibit P200022**, official U.S. District Court catalog: https://coop.vaed.uscourts.gov/moussaoui/exhibits/prosecution/P200022.html . Catalog describes **2 impact clips (1–2), 6 aftermath clips (3–8), 12 JPEG stills**, and a **10m18s MPEG compilation distributed in a ZIP**. Each **P200022A.jpg through P200022L.jpg** was inspected separately. The ZIP bytes could not be examined in this pass, so segment-level testing remains blocked.
5. **United States v. Moussaoui, stipulation ST00004**, official prosecution PDF, **p. 9 ¶26**: https://coop.vaed.uscourts.gov/moussaoui/exhibits/prosecution/ST00004.pdf . Describes a single impact clip as 'clip 1' and **'seven video clips (clips 2–7)'** depicting aftermath. The latter range contains six labels. Party stipulation authenticates the proffered trial exhibit under its terms, not a full chain back to native recorder storage.
6. ***Judicial Watch v. FBI*, 522 F.3d 364**, D.C. Circuit **April 11, 2008**, court PDF **pp. 2–4**, https://media.cadc.uscourts.gov/opinions/docs/2008/04/07-5158-1110396.pdf . Documents FOIA 7(A) history and court-ordered releases; identifies the **Nexxcom/Citgo** tape. Its disclosure history is not evidence-item custody.
7. **Historical DoD FOIA register**, entry **12-F-0979**, received **June 8, 2012**, seeking materials described on Sep. 12 receipt; historical locator only, NOT the user's pending request number: https://documents.theblackvault.com/documents/foia/OSDJSLogs-2009-2013.pdf . Log entry does not prove a release or item disposition.
8. **Original Austin/Pennington 2006 oral history:** specifically sought but full authenticated transcript not acquired. Official locator: https://history.defense.gov/Portals/70/Documents/oral_history/OH_Trans_AustinBrian%20Steve%20Pennington111-9-2006.pdf . Technical secondhand quotations about native sampling, DAT copies or timestamp bugs are not adopted as established findings.

## Preserved 85-record arithmetic

| Count | Review category | Evidentiary qualification |
|---:|---|---|
| 56 | Excluded on chain-of-custody/supporting documentation | Not 56 tapes personally watched by Maguire |
| 16 | Viewed, no crash-site view | **Not shown to be the FD-597 receipt's 16 tapes** |
| 12 | Viewed, showed crash site only after impact | Could include many cameras, times or bundled records; no source-level index here |
| 1 | Impact-imagery record | Maguire ¶4 says its released CD-ROM contains **two time-lapse recordings** |
| **85** | **Total 56+16+12+1** | Search pool of potentially responsive media, not 85 verified approach views |

The FBI's FOIA search initially reported no responsive video and corrected the finding in 2005. The December 2006 court approved the adequacy of its particular search, not every historical collection, recording or disposal.

## Receipt-to-release ledger

**Status definitions:** *Verified* means the cited original directly names an event or artifact. *Partial* means part of a chain is documented but its endpoints are not cross-referenced. *Unmatched* means no item-specific join was recovered. A 'release' is a public derivative unless the original recording system and its acquisition can be authenticated.

| Object / group | Original acquisition / receipt | Traceable processing or release | Exact missing link / status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **R01–R16: sixteen surveillance tapes**, individual item labels **not visible** | FBI FD-597 Sep.12, 2001; NCT0073582; Greg Goff; file 265D-NY-280350 | No tape-by-tape forensic accession, camera assignment, transfer or disposition located | **Unmatched:** unique labels, camera IDs, start/end intervals, seizure/inventory seals, return/destruction |
| **R17: CCTVware recorder/digital recording unit, model 951-0008**, described as hard drive | Same FD-597 | No documented forensic image, mount log, unit serial, system configuration or transfer to a named lab | **Unmatched:** physical serial, storage image hash, chain, servicing, ownership and disposition |
| **R18: small gold key** to hard drive | Same FD-597 | No independent key-access or return record | **Unmatched:** custody, whether used and disposal |
| **Maguire's FBI 85-item search pool** | FBI evidence-system search, also direct Quantico submission and Washington Field Office acquisition channels; Maguire signed declaration ¶11 | 56 excluded by paperwork; 29 personally viewed, divided 16/12/1 | **Partial:** individual evidence IDs, acquisition source, review worksheets and disposition for all 85 unavailable |
| **Maguire '16 no crash-site view'** | Only a screening subset, ¶13–14 | Categorized as nonresponsive | **Unmatched to R01–R16:** sharing the number 16 supplies no identifier-level evidence |
| **Responsive CD-ROM containing two time-lapse copies** | Maguire ¶4 attributes to two Pentagon parking-lot cameras | Responsive recording disclosed in litigation; source concept aligned with court exhibit | **Partial:** CD accession, creation/export date, disc hash, parent drive/tape identity and custody links to FD-597 and P200022 |
| **P200022 public courtroom presentation** | Original security recorder not identified in its web catalog | Catalog: 2 impact + 6 aftermath clips; 12 JPEGs and a 10:18 MPEG ZIP | **Partial:** native master/export manifest, segment filenames and checksums, trial exhibit build worksheet |
| **P200022A–L JPEG stills** | Selected from unidentified native source(s) | 12 public image files, visually reviewed; several are zoomed crops at repeated displayed seconds | **Verified as public derivatives**, **unmatched** to native frame IDs and acquisition times |
| **Citgo / Nexxcom recording** | FBI possesses tape; Maguire ¶15 says it was among 85 | FAVIAU reportedly found no impact image; released September 2006 under separate litigation | **Partial:** unique evidence number, intake/transfers, native quality and disposition; no FD-597 join |
| **Doubletree Hotel recording** | FBI possession discussed in declaration and appellate litigation | Public by December 2, 2006; first-party requester describes 37 hours from 10 active camera feeds | **Partial:** direct 85-entry match not demonstrated, nor to FD-597; source tapes and digitization logs absent |
| **Sheraton recording** | No authenticated acquired tape demonstrated | FBI search reported it could not locate a Sheraton tape | **No positive acquisition/release chain:** absence from search does not establish existence or destruction |
| **DoD 12-F-0979** | June 8, 2012 request seeking FD-597-described items | Agency FOIA log entry only | **Not a custody or release record**; not a current user case number |

## P200022 clip-count reconciliation

| Source / layer | Printed text | Implication |
|---|---|---|
| P200022 catalog | Two impact clips 1–2; six aftermath 3–8 | Eight enumerated clip slots |
| ST00004 p.9 ¶26 | One impact clip 1; seven aftermath, described as clips 2–7 | Stated **1+7=8**, but written **2–7** spans six, creating a self-inconsistency |
| Published 12 JPEGs A–L | Still photographs, some labeled zoom and sharing timestamps | **Do not** equal twelve acquired video recordings or clips |
| MPEG ZIP, listed 10:18 | Advertised single compilation | Not inspected here: cannot determine segment boundaries or missing exports |

Possible clerical misnumbering, reclassification of one clip as impact versus aftermath, editing/bundling, or a genuine source-item mismatch are **hypotheses**. No original segment manifest or exhibit-build log was recovered to discriminate. The contradiction alone does not prove deleted or fabricated imagery.

## New, narrow technical conclusions and stopping rules

- The signed Maguire declaration independently removes ambiguity between **one responsive CD-ROM** and **two camera time-lapse recordings**, without identifying the source recorder.
- The trial stills display **September 12, 2001 around 17:37:19–17:37:24**, incompatible on its face with a September 11 event-clock overlay. This is an **observed displayed-date mismatch**, not proof of a particular conversion bug, cause, corrected clock or acquisition cadence.
- Some court stills explicitly identify **zoomed** variants of the same displayed second (for instance A/B, E/F, G/H and I/J), so 12 JPEGs do not represent 12 independent instants.
- P200022's downloadable MPEG **compilation playback metadata**, even if inspected, would **not** identify native camera capture frequency. Those require the native media/frame timing, CCTVware 951-0008 manual/configuration and export software/forensic logs.
- Nothing here establishes a DAT original, 1 frame/second native recording, or software-specific time-offset correction: the Austin/Pennington transcript and relevant technical chain were not authenticated.
- Court-reported absence of a plane in Citgo/Doubletree views has defined coverage limits; unknown other camera contents cannot be promoted to clear approach footage. No authenticated missile footage or missile inference follows from the incomplete custody ledger. The aircraft identity question has other evidence streams (NTSB Flight 77 radar/FDR) independent of these custody gaps.

## Source dependence and strongest competing explanations

- **FD-597 + NCT index**: the index references the receipt; it is **not a second observation** of the 16 tapes.
- **Maguire affidavit + Bingham opinion**: the opinion summarizes the declaration, so the 85 arithmetic has one primary witness/inventory origin. Court approval is legal review, not independent examination of 85 recordings.
- **ST00004 + P200022 catalog + 12 public JPEGs**: all concern the same trial exhibit; they are separate descriptions/presentations, not three independent acquisition sources.
- **Judicial Watch requester account + court appellate chronology**: the former is party reporting; the latter supplies court-documented timing and legal grounds, not source-tape receipt identities.
- **Ordinary media processing explanation**: a responsive compilation assembled from two parking-lot views, with exhibit numbering inconsistencies and exports, is compatible with available documents; **not confirmed** without the build manifest.
- **Omission, misfile, additional copy, or unreleased original**: cannot be excluded solely from absent item crosswalk. Such alternatives need a specific object, its inventory identifier and contrary records.

## Concrete outstanding objects, in priority order

1. **Complete FD-597 NCT0073581–82 scan**, original evidence envelope and a numbered R01–R18 inventory with each tape's labels and receipt signatures.
2. **FD-192/collected-item, chain-of-custody and accession ledgers** for case 265D-NY-280350, including 16 tape items, model 951-0008 hardware, key, and executed transfer/return/destruction dispositions.
3. **Native recorder/drive forensic acquisition**: drive serial, image manifest and hashes, extraction/export timestamps, camera-to-channel map, playback software/model/firmware, clock time zone and adjustment tests.
4. **Original 85-item search returns and Maguire review worksheet**: case identifiers, each tape ID, source, screen result and subsequent FOIA/release status. This alone would test whether the two groups of 16 overlap.
5. **CD-ROM → P200022 exhibit build and release manifest**, including source filenames/hashes, per-clip boundaries, creation dates, and whether the still JPEG crops derive from the same source frames.
6. **Full ST00004/P200022 exhibit assembly file** or verified actual MPEG segment readout, resolving 2+6 versus 1+7 and 'clips 2–7'.
7. **Authentic Austin/Pennington oral-history transcript** with identifiable original system tests and backup media records before assessing 1fps/DAT/export-bug anecdotes.

**Final status:** Important original documents and public derivatives now have clearer descriptions, especially Maguire's signed affidavit. **No affirmative item-to-item FD-597 → evidence accession → native extraction → impact CD-ROM/P200022 → present physical disposition has been recovered.** The forensic-origin and clock/capture-rate gates therefore remain closed. This is absence of a documented public join, not proof that the items are lost or that their contents show an alternative attack mechanism.

No contacts, requests, amendments, purchases, fees, repository changes, or alterations to pending matters were made.
````

````
Calculations and independence rules
The declared ATC composite interval reproduces as 2,639 seconds =43min59s. A four-hour UTC-to-EDT
conversion is a timezone operation and is separate from any recorder-clock correction. No seconds-level
correction was adopted without its original derivation.
Primary radar range/azimuth can be an independent physical measurement after target association and
clock calibration. Transponder altitude can share aircraft air-data sources with the FDR. Tracked/coasted
coordinates add processing and prediction. Repeated plots, NTSB's combined study and the
Commission's synthesis therefore cannot be counted as multiple independent terminal-height/clock
measurements.
The current fetched radar representations differ in byte size from catalog metadata. The DCA difference equals its LF count,
consistent with newline normalization; the IAD difference is one byte larger than its LF count. The handwritten PDF also has
an unresolved representation-size difference. Exact fetched hashes and these limits are preserved in the manifest.

Dependent conclusion
stopped. Required links remain
unresolved.
Positive and contrary evidence retained
The NTSB factual report says the recorder operated normally and identifies a validated group. That is
contrary evidence to treating all recorder values as unreliable. The same report expressly separates
unconfirmed/improperly recorded fields, so its general statement cannot validate every label. The
recovered transmittal and matching file sizes support the archived attachments' provenance, while their
mirror chain and runtime identity remain bounded. [S1 p.2; S6]
Exact missing inputs that stop dependent work
N644AA as-installed wiring/options and sensor-to-bus semantics; the applicable acquisition-unit
interface/control requirements and numeric/discrete failure behavior; the exact producing RAPS/database
configuration or a validated equivalent raw-to-output implementation; channel calibration and
time/latency records; the separate Time Correlation Report; radar dictionary, site/clock/scanner metadata
and target-association chain; independent preceding-flight observations/logs for known sensor states.
None of these missing records is described as withheld, destroyed or never created. No finding here identifies an actor's
tasking, knowledge, action or causal effect. This is a measurement-chain audit.


SCOOBY  /  BRIEF 14 - N03  /  12 NOVEMBER 2026
9
8. Preceding flights and reproducibility
What was acquired from the author-decoder record
The archived output index supplies Dulles, Los Angeles, Chicago and Orlando landing CSVs,
FlightTimes.csv and AllSubframesFltDeckDoor.csv. They derive from the same recovered recorder data
and author software. FlightTimes records 25 sync-loss boundaries with Excel-added interval calculations;
it is not an independent airline log. The landing names and apparent ground segments do not supply
independently observed door states or calibrated runway-height tests. [S12]
The recovered source ZIP has a main project ApplicationVersion 1.10.0.0 and a backup1.7.0.0, while
assembly/file versions remain1.0.0.0. The changelog says version1.1 adjusted acceleration formulas to
match NTSB CSV output,1.4.1 added a pressure-altitude variant based on the TXT, and1.7 added raw
coarse/fine outputs. No output-to-build/input-hash/run-log join was recovered. Agreement achieved by
reference to NTSB output is not independent sensor calibration. No downloaded code was executed.
Package contents
File / directory
Purpose
reproduce.py
Python3 standard-library script. Checks exact CSV/TXT hashes; audits export
occupancy and final cells; extracts relevant database definitions with line
numbers; reproduces specification examples.
calculations.json
Actual script output, including observed CSV cells, derived counts/differences
and separately labeled synthetic/specification examples.
parameter_validation_ledger.json
Machine-readable installation, address, sampling, conversion,
invalid-behavior and validation ledger.
source_manifest.json
Original/capture URLs, archive dates, exact bytes/SHA-256, source classes,
provenance limits and package paths.
sources/
Retrieved official reports, candidate raw/CSV/TXT, FOIA letter scans, official
radar exports and metadata. The Boeing manual is referenced by exact
URL/hash, not republished in full.
unvalidated_derivatives/
Author-decoder preceding-flight and door CSVs, preserved with their
unvalidated status. They are not inputs to the reported physical conclusions.
Run from the extracted package:
python3 reproduce.py --output recalculated.json
The script refuses a different CSV or TXT hash. It does not decompress the raw FDR file, execute author
code, decode radar tracks or calculate an impact trajectory. Review the source paths and missing links
before extending it. The report's calculation table can be reproduced offline using the included inputs.
Source dependence
NTSB raw acquisition, NTSB CSV, Stutt derivatives, the Flight Path Study and the Commission synthesis
are connected source chains, not five independent measurements. The Boeing specification and AA
database are conversion documentation, not aircraft observations. The official radar exports originate
outside the FDR, but need their own field/clock/target validation before comparison. Prior project
summaries guide retrieval and do not replace these originals.
````

````
incomplete. The cockpit-door and radio-height fields remain unvalidated for the physical claims at

Source locators: door [S2 E35/PDF431 and E110/PDF506; S3 lines 8136-8152]; GMT [S2 E21/PDF417; S3 9477-9545]; GPS

NTSB's separate classifications.
What a constant door value can establish
With a correctly decoded slot, constancy establishes only constancy of that recorded value. It cannot
distinguish a steady physical state from an unwired/default, failed, stale or differently configured source.
Only SF3 is assigned to the door by this map; the same word/bit in other subframes is not automatically
another door observation. A known-state transition joined to the same aircraft and channel would be
useful validation, but none was recovered.

Four different altitude quantities
Pressure altitude is tied to a standard pressure reference. Barometrically corrected altitude applies
a specified pressure setting and conversion. Radio height is a sensor-dependent return relative to the
reflecting surface and aircraft geometry. Terrain clearance requires a consistent geometric altitude and
terrain/position reference. The Flight Path Study labels its plot FDR Pressure Altitude, even where the axis
says ft-msl; that wording supplies no missing geometric calibration. [S8 p.4]


SCOOBY  /  BRIEF 14 - N03  /  12 NOVEMBER 2026
5
4. Reproducible specification checks
These checks use the recovered specification and database definitions. They do not decode an observed
flight record. A printout mismatch is an implementation question until the actual converter, database
version, inputs and output are joined. It is not proof that the NTSB output is wrong.
Pressure altitude: overlap and duplicated sign
For the 757-3/-3A/-3B format, Boeing Note3C directs omission of W30 bits 12-10 when combining the two
words. The printout separately describes a signed10 fine field and a computed ALT_COA + ALT_FIN. A
literal implementation of that listing can differ from the specification. [S2 p.20; S3
1737-1769,14212-14223]
coarse = signed10((W29 >> 2) & 1023)
fine = (W30 >> 2) & 127
pressure_ft = 128 * coarse + fine
Example
Boeing rule
Literal printout
sum
Interpretation
Published 34,423 ft
34,423
34,423
This example does not discriminate the
alternatives.
Synthetic 1,000 ft
1,000
1,384
Overlap bits expose the difference. Not a
recovered raw sample.
Synthetic -500 ft
-500
-1,012
Repeated sign also matters. Not a recovered
raw sample.
Barometer setting: input BCD versus recorded count
Boeing's 29.92 inHg example records octal 5660, equal to decimal 2992; dividing by 100 recovers 29.92.
The same recorded bits interpreted directly as three BCD nibbles produce 11,11,0, outside decimal-digit
coding. The TXT calls the field BCD and supplies no visible decimal scale in that entry. The missing link is
the converter's interpretation, not a demonstrated instrument fault. [S2 p.21; S3 2812-2842]
Radio height: sign location and component order
Move W31 bit12 to the signed14 sign position; place W32 bits1-2 in data positions11-12; retain W31's
lower11 data bits. Boeing's two published examples test different failure modes. [S2 p.27]
W31 / W32
Boeing result
Naive contiguous signed14
Signed12 low + high x2048
904 / 2
5,000 ft
-7,288 ft
5,000 ft
3596 / 3
-500 ft
-500 ft
5,644 ft
The downloadable Python script reproduces all these values. Passing one example is not sufficient validation of an
alternative bit assembly. The script also verifies the 3,072 bits/s frame arithmetic; the 3.90625 ms word slot is a
serialization interval, not sensor-to-ground-clock accuracy.
````

````
Source locators: door [S2 E35/PDF431 and E110/PDF506; S3 lines 8136-8152]; GMT [S2 E21/PDF417; S3 9477-9545]; GPS
entries [S3 9566-9633]. The generic diagram joins a bus label to a recorder slot, but not the cockpit switch to that bus on

NTSB's separate classifications.
What a constant door value can establish
With a correctly decoded slot, constancy establishes only constancy of that recorded value. It cannot
distinguish a steady physical state from an unwired/default, failed, stale or differently configured source.
Only SF3 is assigned to the door by this map; the same word/bit in other subframes is not automatically
another door observation. A known-state transition joined to the same aircraft and channel would be
useful validation, but none was recovered.

Four different altitude quantities
Pressure altitude is tied to a standard pressure reference. Barometrically corrected altitude applies
a specified pressure setting and conversion. Radio height is a sensor-dependent return relative to the
reflecting surface and aircraft geometry. Terrain clearance requires a consistent geometric altitude and
terrain/position reference. The Flight Path Study labels its plot FDR Pressure Altitude, even where the axis
says ft-msl; that wording supplies no missing geometric calibration. [S8 p.4]


SCOOBY  /  BRIEF 14 - N03  /  12 NOVEMBER 2026
5
4. Reproducible specification checks
These checks use the recovered specification and database definitions. They do not decode an observed
flight record. A printout mismatch is an implementation question until the actual converter, database
version, inputs and output are joined. It is not proof that the NTSB output is wrong.
Pressure altitude: overlap and duplicated sign
For the 757-3/-3A/-3B format, Boeing Note3C directs omission of W30 bits 12-10 when combining the two
words. The printout separately describes a signed10 fine field and a computed ALT_COA + ALT_FIN. A
literal implementation of that listing can differ from the specification. [S2 p.20; S3
1737-1769,14212-14223]
coarse = signed10((W29 >> 2) & 1023)
fine = (W30 >> 2) & 127
pressure_ft = 128 * coarse + fine
Example
Boeing rule
Literal printout
sum
Interpretation

34,423
34,423

alternatives.

Overlap bits expose the difference. Not a
recovered raw sample.
Synthetic -500 ft
-500
-1,012
Repeated sign also matters. Not a recovered
raw sample.
Barometer setting: input BCD versus recorded count
Boeing's 29.92 inHg example records octal 5660, equal to decimal 2992; dividing by 100 recovers 29.92.
The same recorded bits interpreted directly as three BCD nibbles produce 11,11,0, outside decimal-digit
coding. The TXT calls the field BCD and supplies no visible decimal scale in that entry. The missing link is
the converter's interpretation, not a demonstrated instrument fault. [S2 p.21; S3 2812-2842]
Radio height: sign location and component order
Move W31 bit12 to the signed14 sign position; place W32 bits1-2 in data positions11-12; retain W31's
lower11 data bits. Boeing's two published examples test different failure modes. [S2 p.27]
W31 / W32

The downloadable Python script reproduces all these values. Passing one example is not sufficient validation of an
alternative bit assembly. The script also verifies the 3,072 bits/s frame arithmetic; the 3.90625 ms word slot is a
serialization interval, not sensor-to-ground-clock accuracy.

Limits on the arithmetic
The pressure differences above are differences between published engineering values, not geometric
descent rates near impact. No extrapolation to an impact time was performed. No pressure-setting
adjustment, static-error correction, radio-height substitution or terrain subtraction was silently added.
Those operations require the specific conversion, calibration and time alignment that remain unresolved.
The selected export contains no header for FLT DECK DOOR, RADIO HEIGHT, GMT or GPS TIME. That is a statement about
this export's columns, not sensor installation. A blank cell can reflect the export schedule and must not automatically be
coded as zero, invalid or forward-filled telemetry.

Calculations and independence rules
The declared ATC composite interval reproduces as 2,639 seconds =43min59s. A four-hour UTC-to-EDT
conversion is a timezone operation and is separate from any recorder-clock correction. No seconds-level
correction was adopted without its original derivation.
Primary radar range/azimuth can be an independent physical measurement after target association and
clock calibration. Transponder altitude can share aircraft air-data sources with the FDR. Tracked/coasted
coordinates add processing and prediction. Repeated plots, NTSB's combined study and the
Commission's synthesis therefore cannot be counted as multiple independent terminal-height/clock
measurements.
The current fetched radar representations differ in byte size from catalog metadata. The DCA difference equals its LF count,
consistent with newline normalization; the IAD difference is one byte larger than its LF count. The handwritten PDF also has
an unresolved representation-size difference. Exact fetched hashes and these limits are preserved in the manifest.

Positive and contrary evidence retained
The NTSB factual report says the recorder operated normally and identifies a validated group. That is
contrary evidence to treating all recorder values as unreliable. The same report expressly separates
unconfirmed/improperly recorded fields, so its general statement cannot validate every label. The
recovered transmittal and matching file sizes support the archived attachments' provenance, while their
mirror chain and runtime identity remain bounded. [S1 p.2; S6]
Exact missing inputs that stop dependent work
N644AA as-installed wiring/options and sensor-to-bus semantics; the applicable acquisition-unit
interface/control requirements and numeric/discrete failure behavior; the exact producing RAPS/database
configuration or a validated equivalent raw-to-output implementation; channel calibration and
time/latency records; the separate Time Correlation Report; radar dictionary, site/clock/scanner metadata
and target-association chain; independent preceding-flight observations/logs for known sensor states.
None of these missing records is described as withheld, destroyed or never created. No finding here identifies an actor's
tasking, knowledge, action or causal effect. This is a measurement-chain audit.


SCOOBY  /  BRIEF 14 - N03  /  12 NOVEMBER 2026
9
8. Preceding flights and reproducibility
What was acquired from the author-decoder record
The archived output index supplies Dulles, Los Angeles, Chicago and Orlando landing CSVs,
FlightTimes.csv and AllSubframesFltDeckDoor.csv. They derive from the same recovered recorder data
and author software. FlightTimes records 25 sync-loss boundaries with Excel-added interval calculations;
it is not an independent airline log. The landing names and apparent ground segments do not supply
independently observed door states or calibrated runway-height tests. [S12]
The recovered source ZIP has a main project ApplicationVersion 1.10.0.0 and a backup1.7.0.0, while
assembly/file versions remain1.0.0.0. The changelog says version1.1 adjusted acceleration formulas to
match NTSB CSV output,1.4.1 added a pressure-altitude variant based on the TXT, and1.7 added raw
coarse/fine outputs. No output-to-build/input-hash/run-log join was recovered. Agreement achieved by
reference to NTSB output is not independent sensor calibration. No downloaded code was executed.
Package contents
File / directory
Purpose
reproduce.py
Python3 standard-library script. Checks exact CSV/TXT hashes; audits export
occupancy and final cells; extracts relevant database definitions with line
numbers; reproduces specification examples.
calculations.json
Actual script output, including observed CSV cells, derived counts/differences
and separately labeled synthetic/specification examples.
parameter_validation_ledger.json
Machine-readable installation, address, sampling, conversion,
invalid-behavior and validation ledger.
source_manifest.json
Original/capture URLs, archive dates, exact bytes/SHA-256, source classes,
provenance limits and package paths.
sources/
Retrieved official reports, candidate raw/CSV/TXT, FOIA letter scans, official
radar exports and metadata. The Boeing manual is referenced by exact
URL/hash, not republished in full.
unvalidated_derivatives/
Author-decoder preceding-flight and door CSVs, preserved with their
unvalidated status. They are not inputs to the reported physical conclusions.
Run from the extracted package:
python3 reproduce.py --output recalculated.json
The script refuses a different CSV or TXT hash. It does not decompress the raw FDR file, execute author
code, decode radar tracks or calculate an impact trajectory. Review the source paths and missing links
before extending it. The report's calculation table can be reproduced offline using the included inputs.
Source dependence
NTSB raw acquisition, NTSB CSV, Stutt derivatives, the Flight Path Study and the Commission synthesis
are connected source chains, not five independent measurements. The Boeing specification and AA
database are conversion documentation, not aircraft observations. The official radar exports originate
outside the FDR, but need their own field/clock/target validation before comparison. Prior project
summaries guide retrieval and do not replace these originals.
````
