### 31. R20-—-WTC-Access-Data-Provenance-—-Research-Result-(Nov-12,-2026).txt

- Type: Project uploaded result file
- Bytes: 7464
- SHA-256: `aa415e717b9564fe9f1261c0b17f08395ff6b70a058bed6d81c362bfaf6a71b2`
- Original payload: <https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/scooby-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/research/scooby-chatgpt-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/project-source-files/R20-%E2%80%94-WTC-Access-Data-Provenance-%E2%80%94-Research-Result-%28Nov-12%2C-2026%29.txt>

````
Scooby Investigation 10 — WTC access-data provenance

Status: Unresolved. Individual access comparisons remain blocked.

The November 11 index identifies this as a provenance investigation that can proceed using public records. I located the earlier Deep Research initiation notice, but no completed Investigation 10 report in the accessible project materials. The remaining work is therefore the documentary custody comparison, not another general investigation of WTC security.

Two original government publications establish distinct historical data transfers:

NIST, September 2005: Port Authority badge-holder lists were used to construct a survey sampling frame for WTC 1 and WTC 2. They were not September 11 entry logs. 
NCSTAR 1-7

NYC DOHMH/RTI, April 2006 manual: A separate transfer in May 2004 contained 95,442 people issued security badges for one or more of the seven WTC buildings during the preceding five years. The manual does not establish temporary-pass coverage or specific visits. 
Data File User's Manual 04-24-06.doc

A third route—an E-J Electric subcontractor's reported offsite security-database backup and FBI download—requires separate examination. It potentially concerns different records from either badge roster.

Dataset coverage and custody joins

Route and original source

	

Data type and established coverage

	

Custody evidence

	

Missing join




PA → NIST, May and August 2003. NIST status report, July 21, 2004; NCSTAR 1-7, §3.2.2, pp. 50–52.

	

Badge-authorized occupants of WTC 1/2; name, floor, employer and SSN reported.

	

Two deliveries acknowledged by NIST.

	

Original transmittals, filenames, snapshot dates, schema, version differences and disposition.




PA → DOHMH → RTI, May 2004. WTCHR manual, §4.5.3, p. 25.

	

95,442 badge recipients, all seven buildings, approximately five-year period.

	

RTI's receipt documented in the joint agency manual.

	

PA→DOHMH handoff, original file/schema, pass categories, individual-building coverage and disposition.




E-J subcontractor → FBI, reported September 2001. EC&M, September 20 and October 1.

	

Offsite security database reportedly included photographs and personnel information. Event-data inclusion unverified.

	

E-J executives described an FBI download in California.

	

FBI acquisition receipt, subcontractor identity, media/file identifiers, extraction date, schema, event coverage and final custody.




PA/Reiss → researcher, March 2005. Murphy, AJPH, January 2009, pp. 66–67.

	

14,417 tower card accesses on May 8, 2001, midnight–8:47 a.m.

	

Research publication attributes figure to written communication.

	

Original communication, query, distinct-person rule, input rows and retention.

The first two routes are documented organizational transfers; neither is an event-level dataset. The E-J route has an attributed first-person transfer account, but no verified FBI accession. The March 2005 route documents a reported historical aggregate, not the continued existence of its underlying transactions. 
NIST
+4

E-J: the consequential unresolved distinction

A newly checked original is Amy Florence Fischbach's September 20, 2001 EC&M article. E-J president Tony Mann said ten FBI agents had visited a California subcontractor to download the company's security database. The October 1, 2001 version repeats that account and adds vice-president Jim Usher's confirmation that the information was backed up at a remote location. These are not two independent FBI receipts; they depend on E-J representatives interviewed by the same publication. 
EC&M
+1

The January 1, 2001 technical description says cards could be associated with particular turnstiles and entry times, but explicitly says departures were not tracked. A July 1, 2002 article instead describes 18 months of historical entry-and-exit information. This is a genuine source-description discrepancy, not proof that departure records existed or were delivered to the FBI. 
EC&M
+1

The recovery of a database, even one containing visitor photographs, does not by itself establish its exact fields, the last successful backup, completeness on September 11 or current availability.

Existing requests and productions

I checked the available Port Authority correspondence. The principal records gaps are already covered by requests sent November 6, 2026.

The badge-list request expressly covers the two NIST deliveries, the DOHMH/RTI material, transmittals, metadata, field definitions, version histories and disposition records.

The separate visitor/moving-access request covers issuance, temporary credentials, reader events, Zim-related dock and elevator records, FBI exchanges and the March 2005 Reiss communication. A November 9 follow-up supplies the FBI visitor-card receipt locator without replacing the original request.

One Port Authority acknowledgment assigns PRA 202614031 to a request received November 5 and extends the anticipated response to February 15, 2027. The acknowledgment does not identify its request scope sufficiently to assign that number to either November 6 request.

I found no substantive Port Authority production or determination resolving those two requests.

Evidentiary assessment and stopping rule

The evidence supports three distinct propositions:

Historical badge information was transferred and used after September 11. This is established for the NIST and Registry research routes.

An offsite WTC security backup was reportedly accessed by the FBI. Contemporaneous first-person accounts support the report, but the actual acquired dataset is unidentified.

An individual's specific preattack entry can be reconstructed from surviving records. This remains unestablished.

The original FBI FD-597 visitor-card description, September 11, 2001, Section 2 p. 81, does not establish a usable card identifier, complete date, authenticated holder or building. The previously examined property records do not complete that identification. Likewise, the reviewed records identify no authenticated UMS/Max WTC moving transaction.

Consequently, no individual comparison is currently justified. A missing name from NIST's tower authorization roster could reflect an inapplicable credential category, different building, time coverage or identifier, rather than absence of a visit. Conversely, a matching name would establish authorization or issuance within the dataset's actual scope, not necessarily entry.

The remaining decisive custody records are the original NIST and RTI incoming-file inventories and schemas, the California backup's acquisition and FBI receipt, and the Reiss communication with its underlying extraction definition. Neither the existence of a security system nor its reported capacity proves those datasets survive.

E-J's own project account identifies it as prime contractor with subordinate engineering and integration roles. Stratesec's earlier project closeout and any continuing maintenance work must remain separate from operational control or specific access actions.

Final status: Provenance partially documented; individual-event coverage, FBI acquisition details and present custody unresolved. The appropriate action is to preserve the existing requests and stop dependent identity, moving-access and operational-role tests until the missing records or an authenticated equivalent become available.

No requests, contacts, payments or pending-case changes were made.
````

````
What a constant door value can establish
With a correctly decoded slot, constancy establishes only constancy of that recorded value. It cannot
distinguish a steady physical state from an unwired/default, failed, stale or differently configured source.
Only SF3 is assigned to the door by this map; the same word/bit in other subframes is not automatically
another door observation. A known-state transition joined to the same aircraft and channel would be
useful validation, but none was recovered.

Four different altitude quantities
Pressure altitude is tied to a standard pressure reference. Barometrically corrected altitude applies
a specified pressure setting and conversion. Radio height is a sensor-dependent return relative to the
reflecting surface and aircraft geometry. Terrain clearance requires a consistent geometric altitude and
terrain/position reference. The Flight Path Study labels its plot FDR Pressure Altitude, even where the axis
says ft-msl; that wording supplies no missing geometric calibration. [S8 p.4]

4. Reproducible specification checks
These checks use the recovered specification and database definitions. They do not decode an observed
flight record. A printout mismatch is an implementation question until the actual converter, database
version, inputs and output are joined. It is not proof that the NTSB output is wrong.
Pressure altitude: overlap and duplicated sign
For the 757-3/-3A/-3B format, Boeing Note3C directs omission of W30 bits 12-10 when combining the two
words. The printout separately describes a signed10 fine field and a computed ALT_COA + ALT_FIN. A
literal implementation of that listing can differ from the specification. [S2 p.20; S3
1737-1769,14212-14223]
coarse = signed10((W29 >> 2) & 1023)
fine = (W30 >> 2) & 127
pressure_ft = 128 * coarse + fine
Example
Boeing rule

raw sample.
Barometer setting: input BCD versus recorded count
Boeing's 29.92 inHg example records octal 5660, equal to decimal 2992; dividing by 100 recovers 29.92.
The same recorded bits interpreted directly as three BCD nibbles produce 11,11,0, outside decimal-digit
coding. The TXT calls the field BCD and supplies no visible decimal scale in that entry. The missing link is
the converter's interpretation, not a demonstrated instrument fault. [S2 p.21; S3 2812-2842]
Radio height: sign location and component order
Move W31 bit12 to the signed14 sign position; place W32 bits1-2 in data positions11-12; retain W31's
lower11 data bits. Boeing's two published examples test different failure modes. [S2 p.27]

The downloadable Python script reproduces all these values. Passing one example is not sufficient validation of an
alternative bit assembly. The script also verifies the 3,072 bits/s frame arithmetic; the 3.90625 ms word slot is a
serialization interval, not sensor-to-ground-clock accuracy.

09:37:44 EDT. This does not establish that all raw memory ends there, or that impact occurred then. The
two subsequent empty labels do not demonstrate two additional measurement seconds. [S1 p.4; S4]

Limits on the arithmetic
The pressure differences above are differences between published engineering values, not geometric
descent rates near impact. No extrapolation to an impact time was performed. No pressure-setting
adjustment, static-error correction, radio-height substitution or terrain subtraction was silently added.
Those operations require the specific conversion, calibration and time alignment that remain unresolved.
The selected export contains no header for FLT DECK DOOR, RADIO HEIGHT, GMT or GPS TIME. That is a statement about
this export's columns, not sensor installation. A blank cell can reflect the export schedule and must not automatically be
coded as zero, invalid or forward-filled telemetry.

Calculations and independence rules
The declared ATC composite interval reproduces as 2,639 seconds =43min59s. A four-hour UTC-to-EDT
conversion is a timezone operation and is separate from any recorder-clock correction. No seconds-level
correction was adopted without its original derivation.
Primary radar range/azimuth can be an independent physical measurement after target association and
clock calibration. Transponder altitude can share aircraft air-data sources with the FDR. Tracked/coasted
coordinates add processing and prediction. Repeated plots, NTSB's combined study and the
Commission's synthesis therefore cannot be counted as multiple independent terminal-height/clock
measurements.
The current fetched radar representations differ in byte size from catalog metadata. The DCA difference equals its LF count,
consistent with newline normalization; the IAD difference is one byte larger than its LF count. The handwritten PDF also has
an unresolved representation-size difference. Exact fetched hashes and these limits are preserved in the manifest.

Positive and contrary evidence retained
The NTSB factual report says the recorder operated normally and identifies a validated group. That is
contrary evidence to treating all recorder values as unreliable. The same report expressly separates
unconfirmed/improperly recorded fields, so its general statement cannot validate every label. The
recovered transmittal and matching file sizes support the archived attachments' provenance, while their
mirror chain and runtime identity remain bounded. [S1 p.2; S6]
Exact missing inputs that stop dependent work
N644AA as-installed wiring/options and sensor-to-bus semantics; the applicable acquisition-unit
interface/control requirements and numeric/discrete failure behavior; the exact producing RAPS/database
configuration or a validated equivalent raw-to-output implementation; channel calibration and
time/latency records; the separate Time Correlation Report; radar dictionary, site/clock/scanner metadata
and target-association chain; independent preceding-flight observations/logs for known sensor states.
None of these missing records is described as withheld, destroyed or never created. No finding here identifies an actor's
tasking, knowledge, action or causal effect. This is a measurement-chain audit.

9
8. Preceding flights and reproducibility
What was acquired from the author-decoder record
The archived output index supplies Dulles, Los Angeles, Chicago and Orlando landing CSVs,
FlightTimes.csv and AllSubframesFltDeckDoor.csv. They derive from the same recovered recorder data
and author software. FlightTimes records 25 sync-loss boundaries with Excel-added interval calculations;
it is not an independent airline log. The landing names and apparent ground segments do not supply
independently observed door states or calibrated runway-height tests. [S12]
The recovered source ZIP has a main project ApplicationVersion 1.10.0.0 and a backup1.7.0.0, while
assembly/file versions remain1.0.0.0. The changelog says version1.1 adjusted acceleration formulas to
match NTSB CSV output,1.4.1 added a pressure-altitude variant based on the TXT, and1.7 added raw
coarse/fine outputs. No output-to-build/input-hash/run-log join was recovered. Agreement achieved by
reference to NTSB output is not independent sensor calibration. No downloaded code was executed.
Package contents
File / directory
Purpose
reproduce.py
Python3 standard-library script. Checks exact CSV/TXT hashes; audits export
occupancy and final cells; extracts relevant database definitions with line
numbers; reproduces specification examples.
calculations.json
Actual script output, including observed CSV cells, derived counts/differences
and separately labeled synthetic/specification examples.
parameter_validation_ledger.json
Machine-readable installation, address, sampling, conversion,
invalid-behavior and validation ledger.
source_manifest.json
Original/capture URLs, archive dates, exact bytes/SHA-256, source classes,
provenance limits and package paths.
sources/
Retrieved official reports, candidate raw/CSV/TXT, FOIA letter scans, official
radar exports and metadata. The Boeing manual is referenced by exact
URL/hash, not republished in full.
unvalidated_derivatives/
Author-decoder preceding-flight and door CSVs, preserved with their
unvalidated status. They are not inputs to the reported physical conclusions.
Run from the extracted package:
python3 reproduce.py --output recalculated.json
The script refuses a different CSV or TXT hash. It does not decompress the raw FDR file, execute author
code, decode radar tracks or calculate an impact trajectory. Review the source paths and missing links
before extending it. The report's calculation table can be reproduced offline using the included inputs.
Source dependence
NTSB raw acquisition, NTSB CSV, Stutt derivatives, the Flight Path Study and the Commission synthesis
are connected source chains, not five independent measurements. The Boeing specification and AA
database are conversion documentation, not aircraft observations. The official radar exports originate
outside the FDR, but need their own field/clock/target validation before comparison. Prior project
summaries guide retrieval and do not replace these originals.
````

````
# Scooby Investigation 15 — DEA art-student draft: source-span incident register and bounded dispositions

**Desk audit, 12 November 2026.** Source: [DEA, *Suspicious Activities Involving Israeli Art Students at DEA Facilities*, public 60-page reproduction](https://antiwar.com/rep/DEA_Report_redactedxx.pdf). The face of this draft has no signed or dated cover. “June 2001” is a reported date, not a verified cover date. This is a register of **source narrative clusters** (not independent incidents or proved intelligence operatives). A range may contain several incidents and several paragraphs may narrate the same incident. Separate citations within each range use the source's own paragraph numbers; do not infer PDF page from paragraph number.

**Source transcription caution:** The web-readable mirror was used to navigate the scanned original and may expose text hidden by defective electronic redactions. Only identities visible on the original reproduction are intended; no guessed or apparently de-redacted agent identities are relied upon. Minor spelling variants in the transcription are not resolved as identity matches.

**Coverage:** All canonical paragraph numbers 1–182 are represented by at least one row below. Paragraph 19 is not actually printed: the Caribbean section records “No response.” Paragraph 101 is likewise not printed: an unnumbered Little Rock visit follows ¶100. Paragraph 75 is reused for two different cases; the Houston section reuses 70. These are preserved. **The draft's 125-entry person/organization indexing section is a separate derivative cross-reference, not 125 additional incidents**. Multiple unnumbered visits embedded within a numbered paragraph are aggregated here where names or precise separation are missing; an exact unique-incident count is therefore not possible from this draft alone.

Every source-chain column refers to a field-office report *as repeated in the DEA compilation*, unless an actual underlying report is explicitly acquired. “Direct” means that the described source was a claimed participant/observer, not that this review obtained the original signed statement. No later adjudication is independently verified unless specified.

## Three case histories: provenance and stopping points

**Orlando — ¶¶95–100, [original pp.31–32](https://antiwar.com/rep/DEA_Report_redactedxx.pdf#page=31).** On May 2, a DEA employee described a maroon Mitsubishi photographing the building, but did not obtain plate/driver identification. On May 3, Peer Segalovitz was interviewed by DEA and the FBI, and INS reportedly arrested him for unauthorized employment on a B-2 visa. The separate Akyuz Shmuel Sagiv interview ended in release. Another maroon-car observation at 1:30 p.m. did not identify the vehicle as the first car or associate either with a named seller. The opening's assertion of MDMA-related telephone returns is **not joined to a readable caller, account, number or DEA case number**. The **already-sent November 5, 2026 Orlando DEA FOIA request** covers the original encounter notes, phone-trace inputs and returns, headquarters coordination, negative results, and actual disposition. The available sent thread has no substantive DEA production. **Stop:** no independently established MDMA match or operational-tasking link.

**Miami FPS — first ¶75, [original p.25](https://antiwar.com/rep/DEA_Report_redactedxx.pdf#page=25).** The DEA compilation reproduces an FPS report dated February 9 identifying Ohad Aharon and Yafit Segal, solicitation tickets and separate citations for counterfeit Social Security documents, and their release. Segal described a for-profit art-selling business (Universal Art) and subsequent independent selling, an ordinary commercial explanation for the paintings without explaining the false documents. It says a State Department matter was open, **not that the citations were adjudicated or that espionage was found**. The subsequent second paragraph numbered 75 concerns different people on February 15 and is not corroboration of this case. A **separate November 9, 2026 DEA/FPS request already exists** concerning Miami FPS/Universal Art; no new request is made. **Stop:** no FPS citation identifiers or State Department case number supplied.

**Volk Field — ¶¶178–182, [original pp.50–51](https://antiwar.com/rep/DEA_Report_redactedxx.pdf#page=50).** On May 19, Tsvi Watermann and Gal Kantor reportedly sought permission to visit the base museum, then were observed on an active runway. Documents and vehicle rental identify them, and a developed film reportedly showed a person in the car on the runway, with **no aircraft images**. The compilation records sheriff handling, misdemeanor trespass and payment of $210 fines. Its tourism explanation is attributed to their interviews; restricted-area entry and ordinary tourism are not mutually exclusive. **Stop:** no original photographic set, incident reference, county charging number, or signed payment/disposition was recovered. An official disposition is **reported by the DEA draft**, not independently established from court records.

## Exact missing-disposition and document-history list

1. **Whole draft:** originating dated cover/routing slip; author and approving authority; version history; attached interoffice memoranda; dissemination list and agency recipients; any amended/approved final compilation; formal closure or assessment memorandum.
2. **Orlando:** case/opening number and headquarters-coordination serial; original May 2–3 memoranda and FBI/INS referrals; identifiable art-seller phone-number list and custody, subscriber records, database search parameters, MDMA investigations' case identifiers, match or nonmatch result, final disposition and agency acknowledgments. Existing November 5 request is broad enough to cover these categories; no new duplicate request is justified.
3. **Miami FPS:** February 9 FPS originating report and its citation numbers; photocopies/forensics of counterfeit cards and their reported supplier; Universal Art interview documents and commercial records; State Department file/response, final ticket and immigration dispositions. Existing November 9 request should first be reconciled with any future production.
4. **Volk Field:** May 19 ANG security/incident reference, gate authorization, security escort/radio logs, original contact statements, film and photographic contact sheets, Juneau County sheriff booking/citation numbers, court docket and proof of the $210-per-person disposition.
5. **Other referenced referrals:** Montgomery FBI follow-up to ¶¶16–17; Tampa FPS/OSI transmittals under ¶82; Dallas INS and Ladue court dispositions under ¶¶39–52; Richmond Henrico magistrate disposition under ¶¶131–132; Tinker FBI debrief and INS departure disposition under ¶¶175–177. These are potential existing-record identifiers, **not a license to broaden the three selected case histories**.

## Dependence, contrary evidence and six-link attribution test

* **Affiliation:** Passport, visa, current employer, art-company address and reported military service can establish a person's background or a commercial connection. They do not identify an intelligence employer.
* **Tasking:** None of the three selected cases exposes an authenticated instruction, handler, intelligence account or operational order.
* **Access:** Orlando/ Miami show agency-area solicitations; Volk shows actual runway entry, independently distinguishable from a completed intelligence operation. WTC/UMS access is not established by any of these.
* **Knowledge:** No acquired contemporaneous record links any identified person in the three selected cases to a named hijacker, the 9/11 operational plan or a specific foreknowledge statement.
* **Action:** Commercial sales, document possession, restricted-area conduct and immigration offenses are documented at differing evidentiary strengths; no verified intelligence product is recovered.
* **Effect:** No demonstrated compromise of a DEA investigation, air-base defense activity or 9/11 prevention has been joined to these incidents.

**Positive alternatives and limitations:** The Orlando drug-number association remains a specific unverified statement, not automatically disproved. The widely cited 2003 DEA testimony on an international MDMA investigation commencing in Miami in July 2001 concerns a separately described operation; it has no recovered shared case/person/phone identifier with Orlando. The 2002 FBI/DOJ public denials are official retrospective positions but not publicly reconstructed incident-by-incident evidence tests. Conversely, the draft's repeating field narratives, agency alerts and later press descriptions cannot be counted as independent observers.

**Review boundary:** This desk audit inspected the supplied 60-page public scan and a derivative text rendering and checked specified public records and the already-sent Orlando request. It has **not** acquired original agency case jackets, a signed final version, new private correspondence or paid records. No external contacts, new FOIA submissions, fees or changes to pending matters occurred. This is not a new paid Deep Research run.
````

### 35. R18-—-Audit-Five-Claims-—-Deep-Research-Result-(Nov-12,-2026).txt

- Type: Project uploaded result file
- Bytes: 11783
- SHA-256: `f9ef1b9a8d6c7b221c682788c31890637633c59ffb6bc741e9846208132a1c12`
- Original payload: <https://github.com/AveLyra/911-conspiracy-investigation/blob/scooby-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/research/scooby-chatgpt-project-reconciliation-2026-11-12/project-source-files/R18-%E2%80%94-Audit-Five-Claims-%E2%80%94-Deep-Research-Result-%28Nov-12%2C-2026%29.txt>

````
:
Scooby N06 — Five-claim Commission evidence audit

Prior-work status: The November 11 brief 16 and N06 identify this investigation, and an earlier research session was initiated. I found its initiation record but no completed five-claim report to reuse.

1. Preselected claims

I selected five propositions before evaluating their evidentiary outcomes. The criteria were explanatory importance, identifiable Commission footnotes, source dependence, and the availability of evidence capable of testing the claim independently.

#

	

Precise proposition

	

Selection rationale




1

	

Khalid Sheikh Mohammed proposed the aircraft-as-weapons operation to Bin Laden in 1996.

	

Establishes the plot's origin; heavily detainee-dependent.




2

	

Bin Laden approved the planes operation in late 1998 or early 1999.

	

Establishes leadership authorization and timing; heavily detainee-dependent.




3

	

Al-Qaeda financed the 19 hijackers, with no credible evidence of substantial U.S.-based financial assistance.

	

Financial attribution can be tested against transactions rather than testimony alone.




4

	

Bayoumi's February 2000 encounter with Hazmi and Mihdhar was apparently coincidental, rather than demonstrated operational assistance.

	

Tests a consequential inference against documentary evidence and subsequent investigations.




5

	

NEADS received its first military notification concerning American 11 at 8:37:52 a.m. on September 11.

	

Tests a precise operational claim against contemporaneous communications rather than detainee reporting.

The Commission's own methodological warning, printed page 146, establishes that its researchers could review intelligence reports but could not directly interview detainees or their interrogators. This is a documented limitation on the first two claims, not evidence that their accounts were necessarily false. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

2. Findings from the original footnotes

The original notes establish two important qualifications.

First, multiple interrogation reports are not necessarily multiple independent witnesses. Chapter 5 note 35 identifies KSM interrogations dated July 12 and November 6, 2003, alongside accounts attributed to Abu Zubaydah and Khallad. The Commission explicitly acknowledged that neither of the latter two claimed to witness KSM's original 1996 proposal. Thus, their accounts cannot independently authenticate that meeting. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

Second, chapter 5 note 38 expressly acknowledges inconsistent KSM accounts of whether Bin Laden approved the operation in late 1998 or early 1999. The underlying reports are identified as August 18, 2003; January 9, February 19 and April 3, 2004. Repetition across those dates is a single-witness series, not four independent confirmations. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

Correction to the fourth preselected claim: The Commission did not conclude that Bayoumi's restaurant encounter was coincidental. On printed p. 218, it explicitly left chance versus design unresolved. The actual affirmative finding to audit is its separate assessment, on the same page, that it had found no credible evidence Bayoumi knowingly assisted extremist groups. This correction prevents testing an assertion the Commission did not make. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

3. Five-row source-dependency audit

The test here removes contested detainee interrogation reporting, including later summaries derived from it. It does not assume the reporting was false.

Claim

	

Earliest identifiable sources and dependence

	

Finding after source removal




1. KSM's 1996 proposal

	

Report pp. 148–149; chapter 5 notes 33–36. The core account comes from KSM's July 12 and November 6, 2003 interrogations. Abu Zubaydah's May 16, 2003 account and Khallad's April 22, 2004 account are indirect. A separately described 1995 Philippine interrogation concerns an earlier aircraft-attack idea, not proof of the specific 1996 meeting.

	

Specific meeting and proposal remain uncertain. Earlier aircraft-attack concepts survive, but they cannot independently establish KSM's conversation with Bin Laden. 
9-11commission.gov
+1




2. Bin Laden's late-1998/early-1999 approval

	

Report pp. 149–150; chapter 5 notes 16, 36, 38–39. Several dated accounts all originate with KSM. Note 38 acknowledges conflicting dates. No independently authenticated contemporary authorization record was located.

	

Specific approval date and conversation remain uncertain. Later preparation for the operation is compatible with leadership approval, but does not independently date the original decision. 
9-11commission.gov
+1




3. Al-Qaeda financing and absence of substantial domestic financing

	

Report pp. 171–172; chapter 5 note 131. Sources include FBI financial investigations, Adam Drucker interviews, the July 13, 2004 FBI response and KSM/Binalshibh interrogations. The financing monograph draws substantially on the same investigation—not an independent second investigation.

	

Most transaction evidence survives. Overseas transfers, U.S. bank activity, payments for training and approximately $26,000 returned overseas remain supported by financial analysis. Exact total cost, complete ultimate funding attribution and categorical exclusion of other assistance are less secure. 
9-11commission.gov
+1




4. No credible evidence Bayoumi knowingly assisted extremists

	

Report p. 218; chapter 7 notes 15–19. Underlying sources include Bayoumi and Bin Don interviews, an October 8, 2001 FBI interview, hotel records, employment records and FBI assessments. Note 19 also cites KSM's denial of knowing Bayoumi. These are mixed evidence types, not uniformly independent corroboration.

	

The assistance survives; its purpose remains disputed. Later FBI intelligence reporting and the August 2025 judicial findings substantially qualify the 2004 characterization. Neither establishes that Saudi leadership authorized 9/11 or resolves each allegation of operational knowledge. 
9-11commission.gov
+2




5. NEADS first notified at 8:37:52

	

Report p. 20; chapter 1 notes 115–116. Note 115 identifies the FAA's April 19, 2002 transcript, p. 5, plus interviews with Terry Biggio, Collin Scoggins and Daniel Bueno. The Commission publicly played the relevant recorded conversation on June 17, 2004. The transcription and hearing replay concern the same communication.

	

The documented notification survives strongly. The exact claim that no military unit received any earlier notification additionally depends on sufficiently complete records of other communications. The established 8:37:52 call is not itself proof of that universal negative. 
9-11commission.gov
+2

For claim 3, an important correction emerges directly from the financing monograph, Appendix A, printed p. 133: its authors expressly qualify the completeness of the money trail, and footnote 145 challenges a previously reported estimate of additional cash expenditure. An established transaction trail is stronger evidence than an unsupported assumption that every dollar was accounted for. 
Terrorist Financing
+1

4. Interrogation conditions, destruction and editorial influence

The Senate Intelligence Committee's December 9, 2014 study documents coercive interrogation of KSM beginning in March 2003, including waterboarding and subsequently identified fabricated reporting. That provides a concrete reliability concern. It does not establish that every later statement by KSM was false, or determine how any particular interrogation method affected the July 2003–April 2004 reports cited above. An item-level assessment requires the original report, interrogation chronology and relevant corroboration. 
govinfo.gov
+1

The November 2005 destruction of 92 CIA interrogation videotapes was a separate event concerning Abu Zubaydah and Abd al-Rahim al-Nashiri. Released November 8–10 communications document authorization, destruction and subsequent internal reactions. Those tapes must not be described as destroyed recordings of KSM's planning account. 
National Security Archive

On editorial influence, I could establish an actual source-access restriction: Commission researchers were denied direct detainee and interrogator access. The documented effect was reduced ability to assess credibility, clarify answers and control questioning. I did not establish a particular politically motivated draft alteration or fabricated passage. NARA identifies managing-editor files containing report drafts, but the necessary version-to-version comparison was not completed. 
9-11commission.gov
+1

5. Linked originals and archival gaps

The principal inspectable sources for this audit are the July 2004 Commission Report, its original numbered endnotes, the August 2004 terrorist-financing monograph, the December 2014 Senate interrogation study, and August 28, 2025 judicial decision, ECF 11182.

The most consequential missing originals are the individually cited KSM interrogation reports; the complete Bayoumi and Bin Don interview memoranda and relevant financial workpapers; the underlying transaction exhibits for a complete financing reconciliation; and the FAA transcript's full source and timing metadata. Where these are unavailable, the dependent findings above stop at the supported documentary level.

NARA's Commission series finding aid and access guidance identify the appropriate Commission-record route through the Center for Legislative Archives, including record-specific review. The Commission's legislative-branch records are not subject to FOIA. An FBI or CIA copy of a document does not establish identical contents, annotations or custody within the Commission's own archival holdings. 
National Archives
+2

6. Correction log

Item

	

Correction or qualification




C01

	

Replaced the initially selected “coincidental Bayoumi encounter” with the Commission's actual no-credible-evidence finding. The Commission explicitly left chance versus design unresolved.




C02

	

Counted KSM's repeated interrogations as one source lineage, not multiple independent confirmations.




C03

	

Preserved the late-1998/early-1999 discrepancy rather than assigning an unsupported exact approval date.




C04

	

Separated documented financing transactions from estimated total expenditure and ultimate-source attribution.




C05

	

Distinguished confirmation of the 8:37:52 call from proof that no earlier military notification occurred anywhere.




C06

	

Separated destruction of Abu Zubaydah/al-Nashiri recordings from KSM's interrogation reports.




C07

	

Classified the documented Commission access restriction as a research limitation—not proof of editorial fabrication.

Source limitation: The exact Library file titled Pasted markdown.md contains material about a different project, and the specified Loose_Change_Evidence_Review.pdf was not recovered as a directly inspectable PDF in this session. Its prior project findings were available as secondary context, but I did not claim to reread §§1.4 or 6.8. The five-claim audit above therefore rests principally on the verified public originals and remains incomplete wherever the cited underlying records are missing.

Overall result: Two planning-history propositions substantially weaken when detainee reporting is removed. The financing and military-notification propositions retain stronger documentary foundations, but with narrower supported conclusions than categorical readings suggest. Bayoumi's assistance is established; whether it constituted knowingly operational assistance remains contested by subsequent evidence and differing investigative and judicial assessments.

No contacts, submissions, purchases, archival review requests, or changes to pending matters were made.
````

````
# Scooby | Moussaoui version → receipt → review → action audit
**Public-source continuation, 12 November 2026.** Scope: 199M-MP-60130 and related Moussaoui court exhibits, FISA/criminal-warrant decisions, FAA warning, and French information. Page references to court exhibits are **physical PDF pages** unless explicitly marked printed. A court-hosted representation is not a native ACS electronic communication, email attachment, or dispatch acknowledgment.

## Status and provenance

The November 11 follow-up pack, `Scooby_Deep_Research_Followups_2026-11-11.txt`, brief **18**, identifies Exhibit 633 as acquired but unread and directs the version/receipt audit. The accessible project artifact `Moussaoui N09 Investigation.txt` records a research **launch**, not a completed result. A Library/Project search did **not** locate a completed brief-18 report; this bounded search does not establish that no result exists in a separate research session. This continuation uses the seven public source-register items R23–R29 (SHA-identified below), the original court copies and DOJ OIG; it does not claim to be a newly commissioned paid Deep Research run.

The broader *9-11_Foreknowledge_Research_Review.pdf*, November 7, 2026, pp. **3–6** was read as contextual analysis, not treated as an original document or independent receipt evidence. Its emphasis on agency-specific knowledge, FISA threshold uncertainty, the separate criminal route and the local FAA briefing is retained.

### Research-register baseline: previously read and verified repository summaries

All R23–R29 are **project analyses of overlapping originals**, not seven independent confirmations. The source-register descriptions themselves said the underlying originals were not all reacquired in the November 11 review; here decisive court exemplars and the OIG have been checked against the earlier analyses, with remaining reliance labeled.

## New source finding: defense Exhibit 633

[Defense Exhibit 633, physical p.1, Bates `M-MP1-88000402`](https://coop.vaed.uscourts.gov/moussaoui/exhibits/defense/633.pdf#page=1) is a contemporaneous displayed email **August 22, 2001, 10:13 a.m.**, Harry Samit to Charles Frahm and G. Jones, subject **“747 Information.”** The original page image was visually inspected. Its later January 24, 2005 declassification note is not an authoring or delivery date.

Samit says the **opening EC was attached** and had originally been routed to an Iran/Hizballah unit when investigators thought there was a Hizballah connection; by this message he wrote they had **pretty much ruled out** that working theory. He provides named identity and bail-assistance leads in Norman, Oklahoma and Minneapolis and describes a still-contested name match with a Memphis bank robber. Thus the message is not simply a bare terrorist allegation: it contains a correction of an early hypothesis, uncertain identification, and explicit tasks. But the one-page court representation does **not contain the attached EC**; it supplies neither embedded attachment bytes nor recipient acknowledgment or reading log.

It also summarizes **attributed French reporting**: a person connected with a Chechnya group led by al-Khattab and an interview reporting Moussaoui recruited someone to fight there. This is a **source-reported recruitment allegation**, not authenticated original French interview notes, demonstrated formal affiliation with al-Qaeda, or specific advance knowledge of an aircraft attack. The email closes by saying more information may follow, marking the intelligence as incomplete. Retain the exact distinction between attributed source allegation, FBI inference, and proven operational relationship.

A second court representation, [Exhibit 736, p.1](https://coop.vaed.uscourts.gov/moussaoui/exhibits/defense/736.pdf#page=1), reports a written response attributed to Charles Frahm on August 24: beginning name/telephone traces, requesting photographs, and questioning why the French information did not support the proposed foreign-group connection. It supports **receipt of some information by an actual counterpart and an expressed trace intention**. The time labels in the reproduced exchange are internally awkward, and a subject-line case-number digit differs from the `199M-MP-60130` used on other records. Without the native thread and source headers these differences cannot be normalized. The original task returns are not thereby proved. Source wording was examined through the court-file representation and its earlier preserved text; fresh high-resolution PDF capture for Exhibit 736 was not available in this pass.

The table combines **primary court-record observations**, explicitly **retrospective OIG source reconstruction**, and clearly flagged **unresolved joins**. It does not treat an authored lead, address line, court exhibit, recipient acknowledgment, access to another version, oral legal briefing, formal filing and executed search as interchangeable.

## Narrow sensitivity: which missing or altered facts could affect which decision?

**FISA foreign-power review (HQ → NSLU → possible OIPR).** The operative issue was a showing that Moussaoui acted for or on behalf of a qualifying *foreign power*, not simply that his beliefs were extreme or aviation training suspicious. Exs 346, 633 and 693 indicate attributed Chechnya recruitment/Khattab links; Exs349/350 show disagreement over precisely when supporting organizational material should be added. DOJ OIG Ch. 4 §§IV.B.4–6 explains that the more plausible Chechen-group theory and an unnamed-group alternative were not adequately explored; **no one consulted OIPR**, and the four orally briefed NSLU attorneys saw no source documentation. OIG also says Pakistan travel and the interviews about beliefs were omitted from those briefings. The omitted material **could have altered the questions asked, the legal options considered or the readiness to submit**, but **does not prove FISA probable cause or a likely FISA Court approval**. FBI concerns about new/unrecognized foreign-power pleading under pre-9/11 norms were genuine counterconsiderations. [DOJ OIG chapter 4, especially §§IV.B.4–6](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0606/chapter4.htm).

**Accuracy of field/interview editing.** Ex349’s concern over “preparing to fight” versus defensive training, an inadequate versus absent explanation, hearing an alleged plan, and “dangerous one” might have shifted factual weight for an honest legal reviewer. But 693 and redacted 347 do **not identify the exact criticized attachment stage**; some visible clauses already preserve field wording. Maltbie’s email says some material was to be added later and that he corrected wording. The OIG found some edits meaning-changing and also found most stylistic, openly circulated for comments, and attributed to a planned new foreign-power section. Without original interviews, native drafts and the package counsel saw, the chain **stops at a documented dispute and incomplete review**, not proof of a knowingly falsified final application. [Exs349/350/693 as above; DOJ OIG chapter 4, §§IV.C.3–4](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0606/chapter4.htm).

**Criminal-warrant/prosecutor route.** An August 16 Minnesota AUSA reportedly believed the limited facts then presented were close to warrant-ready and offered follow-up; other prosecutors declined to state that a warrant certainly could have issued. The August18 request for OIPR authorization before further criminal contact was an **attempted pathway**, not proof that the pathway was closed by a written refusal. Rowley recommended trying FISA first because of perceived primary-purpose/“smell test” risks; after FISA halted, agents did not revisit the criminal application. A complete affidavit and actual prosecutor review could have altered the opportunity to search the computer; the Sept11 *postattack* criminal warrant is not an independent preattack probable-cause determination. [OIG chapter 4, initial USAO contacts and criminal-warrant analysis](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0606/chapter4.htm).

**FAA notice and possible operational response.** The Minneapolis LHM’s explicit possible 747-takeover assessment and aircraft-controls particulars could have changed the salience of an institutional FAA warning. The Sept4 HQ teletype did not transmit that assessment in the same text, **but local FAA received the teletype and was orally briefed on the threat on Sept5**. It is therefore inaccurate to say FAA universally received no warning. The missing two printed Ex810 pages and both complete dissemination chains matter to *which audience had what* and *what operational measure followed*, not directly to whether a FISA warrant was legally feasible. [Ex810 physical p4/printed6](https://coop.vaed.uscourts.gov/moussaoui/exhibits/defense/810.pdf#page=4); [DOJ OIG chapter 4 §II.H.8](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0606/chapter4.htm).

**French option and field tasks.** A French source offered eventual custody/search, safeguarding evidence from return to Moussaoui; U.S./British sharing was permitted subject to source protection. There was a genuine **August30 plan, August31/Sept4–6 HQ disagreement and September10 headquarters concurrence** on an FBI escort. That is documented progressive follow-through, but not an actual French search before Sept11. Likewise, Oklahoma’s September6 reported **negative identity comparison** constrains the earlier Memphis/Norman lead; without the original Oklahoma response and Memphis return we cannot compute a complete task success score. [Ex59B p4–6; Ex642 p6–7; Ex643 p1](https://coop.vaed.uscourts.gov/moussaoui/exhibits/defense/643.pdf#page=1); [DOJ OIG chapter 4 §§II.E, II.H.7](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0606/chapter4.htm).

## Serial, enclosure and identity control

`199M-MP-60130` is the known Minneapolis case identifier. Readable **court exhibit** identifiers do not themselves establish **native serial** assignment. Observed cross-references are: **serial 3** in 327, 472 handwritten case area candidate and 701; **serial 10** handwritten in 346 and explicitly in Aug30 59B; **serials 13 and 19** in 701, with French information attributed to 19; **serial 16** as an original enclosure reference in 59B. None of these bare references supplies a native serial index, all enclosures, original edit history or recipient access log. Do not conflate redacted/handwritten numbers, case identifiers, Bates production marks, printed pages, and exhibit numbers. Exhibit736's apparent subject-digit discrepancy is held as an unverified variant, not silently reconciled.

Ex810 is a **five-sheet court exhibit whose displayed printed pagination skips 3–4**, not proof anyone destroyed the intervening sheets. Ex59B’s near-blank final physical page does not substitute for any earlier report’s missing page 7. Ex642’s rough translation explicitly marked draft does not identify the final uploaded EC. Ex349/350’s attachment mentions are not recovered attached files. **The French evidence custody sentence contains “not” in both 59B and 642; the earlier contrary reading was an inspection error, not an authentic contradiction.** The *other* “not” in Maltbie’s August28 editing email concerns unrelated draft prose.

## Source dependence, counterevidence and stopping rule

The key primary sources are linked within the table. The Department of Justice Inspector General’s [Chapter Four](https://oig.justice.gov/sites/default/files/archive/special/s0606/chapter4.htm) is a **single retrospective reconstruction** combining interviews and official case records; do not count multiple OIG retellings of the same interview or multiple court exemplars of a single French fax as independent witnesses. Ex327’s repeated jail-call descriptions trace to a single detention log, not 13 connected calls. Ex633 and Ex346 may describe substantially overlapping French reporting, without proving independent source corroboration. Ex59B and Ex642 also derive from the same French supplement and may represent distinct versions; counting both as corroboration is inappropriate.

Contrary evidence remains material: the initial Hizballah lead was discounted; a Memphis suspect match was later ruled out; instructors saw genuine interest in aviation; there were alternative, sometimes contradictory explanations for interviews and travel; pre-9/11 foreign-power standards and practical timing constrained FISA applications; HQ discussed and pursued some additional leads; the local FAA did receive warning and a threat briefing; and the France proposal progressed to HQ escort concurrence. These limit single-cause or motive conclusions. The OIG nevertheless found serious failures, insufficient legal review, some meaning-altering edits, omitted field threat assessment in external teletype, and failure to reconsider the criminal warrant after FISA screening. The evidence supports those particular deficiencies but not a proven plan to let an attack happen.

**Dependent tests stopped:** exact native 633 opening-EC attachment; native LHM attached to 349/350 with full draft/version metadata; final uploaded EC promised in 642, its original translation, and actual delivery; versioned named-recipient NSLU/OIPR/USAO packages with read/decision timestamps; full native serials 3/10/13/16/19 and 1A enclosure manifests; missing 810 printed pages 3–4, complete Aug31 local warning and Sept4 HQ teletype, FAA receipt/action records; original Oklahoma/Memphis task returns and identity evidence; and full contemporaneous France plans, escort clearance/transport/custody/search results. Their absence **prevents package-level conclusions** but proves neither suppression nor misconduct.

**Activity constraints:** Public/held sources only. No contacting anyone, FOIA/records requests, outside fees, uploads, pending-case changes, repository edits or reconstruction of masked identities. No new paid Deep Research job was initiated in this session; this is a public-original/DOJ-OIG continuation using the completed project source reviews.
````

 What the available records can and cannot decide
 The April announcement supports advance planning. Journal of Commerce reported staff and computer systems still at the
 WTC on September 11, challenging an account of complete prior evacuation. Neither fact identifies the carrier or excludes a
 subcontract. [S1, S4]

 Publicly indexed reporting, developer material, released investigative records, historical lease language, court opinions and
 archival catalogs were checked. No original Zim vendor-selection file, moving contract, invoice, bill of lading, subcontractor
 list, job-specific insurance certificate, dock/elevator booking or access log was recovered. The named contractor and
 project-manager leads must not be relabeled as movers.

 Some contemporary articles were available only as later transcriptions; original print pagination was unavailable.
 Redactions, imperfect OCR, blocked mirrors and unexamined private or archival files limit the negative finding. No
 custodians were contacted and no records requests, reproduction orders or paid searches were submitted.

#### Page 1

 RESEARCH DOSSIER



 Loose Change Evidence
 Review
 What is established, what the film overstates, and which questions merit
 further work.

 Research checked through November 6, 2026.



 Several facts behind Loose Change deserve credence. The evidence reviewed does not
 establish its central allegations of planned demolition, substituted aircraft or a
 U.S.-directed attack. Its strongest material concerns warning failures, changing official accounts
 and weaknesses in the investigations. Those issues can be serious even when a proposed
 explanation for them fails.

 This review treats each proposition separately: what happened, how securely it is documented,
 what follows from it, and what additional evidence could change the assessment. A real anomaly
 earns investigation; it does not automatically validate the explanation attached to it.

 The most useful next work concerns documentary accountability and reproducible technical
 analysis. The source register and specific research questions below make those inquiries concrete.
 No new records requests, interviews, laboratory tests or engineering simulations were performed.


   Assessment                   Meaning


   Established narrower         The observation or record is well supported. Its implications must be
   fact                         assessed separately.


   Worth targeted follow-up     A defined gap or conflict remains, with identifiable evidence that could help
                                resolve it.


   Unsupported inference        The cited evidence does not discriminate between the proposed
                                explanation and credible alternatives.


   Contradicted premise         Specific evidence conflicts with a material part of the claim.

#### Page 2

PUBLIC-SOURCE RESEARCH                                                      Facts, inferences and useful follow-up




 Scope and standards
 Which Loose Change?
 The film exists in substantially different editions. For claim attribution, this review checked a
 third-party transcript of the second edition and the distributor's timed transcript for a 52-minute
 version catalogued in 2015. The distributor's cut should not be mistaken for a complete transcript
 of every earlier release. Later technical studies and legal developments are identified as
 subsequent evidence. This is a review of major recurring claims, not a frame-by-frame audit of
                [1][2][3]
 every edition.

 The second-edition text includes the Pentagon and Flight 93, allegedly fabricated calls, surviving
 hijackers, financial trades, security changes and a gold-theft narrative. The distributor's later
 transcript includes the Mineta testimony, red/gray dust chips, WTC 7 witnesses and lost SEC files.
 These anchors establish the scope without assuming that every version retained each allegation.
 [2][3]



 How the evidence is weighed
 Primary records receive priority: recordings and their transcripts, contemporaneous documents,
 audit findings, technical reports, court decisions and original research. Official conclusions are
 evidence to evaluate, not a substitute for the underlying reasoning. Conversely, a critic's
 institutional affiliation, credential or peer-reviewed publication does not by itself establish their
 conclusion.

 Separate four questions: Was an observation real? Was the first explanation accurate? Does the
 alternative explain the complete evidence better? Is there evidence identifying responsible people
 and actions? Confusing these questions is the main source of overstatement in this debate.

 An explanation should survive evidence it did not select: other camera angles, the full recording,
 transaction ownership, contemporary records and plausible competing causes. Missing records
 reduce confidence in what can be reconstructed. They do not tell us what those records would
 have shown.


 1. Failures that deserve scrutiny
 1.1 Warning and information-sharing failures
 ESTABLISHED NARROWER FACT / TARGETED FOLLOW-UP

 The August 6, 2001 presidential brief warned about a domestic bin Laden threat and described
 information consistent with preparations for hijacking or surveillance. It did not identify the date,
 flights or complete September 11 operation. A real warning and detailed advance knowledge of the
 particular attack are different propositions. [47]




LOOSE CHANGE | EVIDENCE REVIEW | NOVEMBER 2026                                                            Page 2

#### Page 3

PUBLIC-SOURCE RESEARCH                                                   Facts, inferences and useful follow-up



 The Justice Department inspector general documented repeated missed opportunities involving
 Khalid al-Mihdhar and Nawaf al-Hazmi, including failures to pass on and act on information about
 their U.S. connections. Its broader review also identified failures involving the Phoenix
 memorandum and the investigation of Zacarias Moussaoui. These findings justify serious criticism
 of prevention and information sharing. [46][49]

 The same inspector-general review disclosed limits on its access to CIA records. The useful
 follow-up is to trace the original intelligence, dissemination decisions and responsibility at each
 missed opportunity. An institution can possess important fragments without anyone possessing the
 completed operational picture; proving deliberate facilitation requires evidence beyond the fact
                                           [46]
 that the fragments were mishandled.

 1.2 The aviation response and changing timelines
 ESTABLISHED NARROWER FACT / TARGETED FOLLOW-UP

 The official aviation chronology changed materially. The DOT inspector general confirmed incorrect
 accounts of when the FAA notified the military about Flight 77 and when an Air Force liaison joined
 a conference call. Three executives learned that the latter account was wrong, yet the correction
 did not reach the Commission. The inspector general recommended considering administrative
 action, while finding insufficient evidence of knowingly false statements or intentional omissions.
 Both parts of that finding matter. [29]

 This supports scrutiny of accuracy and accountability. It does not establish that fighters were
 deliberately held back to permit the attacks. To evaluate that stronger claim, the relevant evidence
 is the recorded sequence of notification, identification, tasking and authorization, not the time
 elapsed since the first television broadcast.

 A separate DOT inspector-general investigation established that an FAA manager destroyed a tape
 containing six controllers' same-day recollections, contrary to retention requirements. This was a
 supplemental witness-account tape, not the original operational air-traffic recordings. Its existence
 was also kept out of the evidence package. This is a documented evidence-preservation failure;
 the report does not establish an order from senior government officials to destroy it. [31]

 1.3 Steel preservation and access to building records
 ESTABLISHED FAILURE; MOTIVE NOT ESTABLISHED

 The March 6, 2002 House hearing documents steel recycled before adequate examination, unclear
 authority to preserve evidence, and delays obtaining drawings. Investigators disagreed about how
 much this impaired the eventual explanation; the hearing does not support saying that no steel
 was examined. These are substantive failures, recorded publicly by Congress. [4]

 The warranted inference is that some opportunities for forensic examination were irreversibly lost.
 Deliberate concealment requires additional evidence of intent. A useful inquiry would reconstruct
 who authorized disposal, which material was screened and retained, and which specific questions
 the losses made unanswerable. Rescue urgency and evidence preservation both belong in that
 assessment.
